Accessibility is built in, not bolted on: every state — Listening, Working, the result, “not recognized” (which tells you what it heard so you can adjust), no speech, or microphone unavailable — has its own earcon and announcement, with successes spoken politely behind your screen reader and failures interrupting; the book is ducked, never paused, so you never lose your place; actual capture starts a beat after the spoken cue so your screen reader’s own voice isn’t recorded; a safety timer closes a forgotten-open microphone; and the menu item’s checkmark mirrors the live listening state. A Type a Voice Command box (Ctrl+Shift+V) remains for when you’d rather not speak aloud. Every voice command also has its own key, so voice is purely additive. (quill/ui/media/voice_capture.py, quill/ui/media/listen_mixin.py, quill/core/media/voice.py, quill/ui/media/voice_control.py)

  • Teach dictation your words — a dictation.md profile. Dictation can now learn your vocabulary. A small, human-editable file called dictation.md in your QUILL data folder has three optional sections: Vocabulary (names, jargon, and acronyms you use — QUILL feeds these to the speech engine as a hint so it spells “wxPython” and “GitHub” your way, using Whisper’s initial-prompt bias, now actually passed through to whisper.cpp), Replacements (spoken-to-written fixes applied to the transcript, one per line as spoken => written, with \n for a line break — great for punctuation macros and names the engine keeps mishearing), and Commands (your own spoken phrases for existing commands, still bound by the safe-command allowlist). The profile applies everywhere dictation transcribes — Locked Dictation, Dictate (Offline), and the Media Player’s hands-free voice — and is a no-op until you make one, so nothing changes for people who don’t want it. The idea is adapted from VS Code’s dictation file, made native to QUILL. (quill/core/speech/dictation_profile.py, quill/core/speech/providers/whispercpp.py, quill/ui/voice_services.py)

  • One hardened home for every secret QUILL holds — and a one-step way to erase it. QUILL keeps a handful of secrets for you: your AI provider keys, remote-site passwords, and the sign-in tokens for any online service you connect. Until now each of those lived in its own small piece of code that reached into the Windows Credential Manager (or the DPAPI-encrypted keys.enc file in portable mode, or the macOS Keychain) on its own. They now all go through a single hardened store, so there is exactly one place that touches your operating system’s vault and one set of rules it always follows: a secret is never written in plain text, never lands in a settings file, a log, or a diagnostic bundle, and is never part of QUILL’s own program files — there is nothing in the app itself for anyone to extract, because the keys and tokens live only in your OS vault, tied to your account. Each service’s secrets are grouped under its own name, so signing out of a service wipes everything it stored in a single step, with nothing left behind. This is quiet groundwork you will not see directly today, but it is the foundation the authenticated online libraries on the roadmap will sign in through. (quill/core/secrets.py)

  • The Book Library can now search the NLS BARD catalogue. QUILL’s Book Library — the one place you search free, accessible reading sources — adds NLS BARD as a source: a free keyword search over the catalogue of the National Library Service for the Blind and Print Disabled (Library of Congress). Pick NLS BARD in the Source list (or leave it on All free sources), search by title, author, or subject, and browse the results with the same fully keyboard- and screen-reader-friendly list, find-in-results, and spoken status as every other source. BARD catalogue results are listings — obtaining a title needs an eligible BARD patron account, done on the BARD website — so each result offers Open in BARD, which opens that title’s official Library of Congress page in your browser to sign in and download there. The search uses BARD’s free public API: no account or key, nothing sent but your search words, and disabled in Safe Mode like every other library source. This is a catalogue-search integration; in-app borrowing of BARD titles is planned for a future release. (Release note: in 1.0.0 the Book Library is a developer-build-only feature — see Release scope above — and is moving to the forthcoming QUILL Social app, so this entry describes it as it stands in a developer build.) (quill/core/library/bard.py, quill/ui/library_dialog.py)

  • The companion apps stop being silent: fourteen new cues for the things you used to have to guess at. Quill Radio, QUILL Cast, Quill Weather and QuillBeacon all announce what they are doing, but announcements arrive as words in a queue — by the time “connecting” has been read to you, the stream has either started or died, and neither one made a sound. Fourteen earcons now mark the moments themselves. Radio cues connecting, the stream genuinely reaching the air, a mid-stream stall, stopping, a stream that failed, a recording starting, a recording saved, and a station added to Favourites — which finally lets you tell “still buffering” from “gone quiet” without asking. Cast cues downloads beginning, each episode landing on disk, and an episode reaching its end. Weather plays its alert cue only for an alert that is genuinely new: never on a routine check, and never a second time for one it has already told you about. QuillBeacon, which had no sound of any kind, cues an item captured and a sync finishing. Every one fires on a real change of state, so a flaky stream that re-buffers ten times in a row makes one sound rather than ten, and queueing forty episodes says “downloading” once. All fourteen are ordinary Sound Events: individually switchable in Tools → Reading & Dictation → Sound Events… and replaceable by any sound pack. And the companion apps now load a sound pack at startup at all — until this release they installed the machinery to play earcons and then had nothing to play them through.

  • Git worktrees: give every branch its own folder, so the document you are reading never changes underneath you. Switching branches the ordinary way rewrites every file in place — same names, same paths, different contents. A sighted user sees the editor change; if you are reading with a screen reader, nothing announces it, and the paragraph under your review cursor is suddenly a paragraph from another branch in a file that still claims to be the file you opened. A worktree fixes that structurally: a second folder attached to the same repository, with a different branch checked out in it, so switching context becomes “open a different file” — something you do and hear — instead of “this file is now a different file”. Tools > Local Git > Worktrees… lists every one of them, each row read as a full sentence (“Linked worktree at S:\code\quill-feature-x, on branch feature/x, locked: on a USB drive”) rather than columns you arrow across, and announces the count on open and the outcome of every action. New Worktree… takes a folder (pasted Explorer quotes, %APPDATA% paths and file:// links all accepted), a branch, or a brand-new branch with an optional starting point — and refuses in plain language before running git when the folder already has files, sits inside the repository, or the branch is already checked out somewhere else, naming the folder that has it. Open in QUILL opens the same document from the worktree you chose, or offers a picker rooted there when that file does not exist on that branch. Remove… confirms with No as the default; if git refuses because the worktree still holds uncommitted work, QUILL passes the refusal on and asks a second question before discarding anything — it never adds --force on your behalf. Lock/Unlock protects a worktree on a USB drive or a network share from being pruned, with a reason you hear later in the list, and Prune tidies up records for folders that are genuinely gone and says which ones. Git’s raw stderr is never spoken at you. (core/git_worktree.py, ui/git_worktree_dialogs.py, ui/main_frame_worktrees.py)

  • Every background watcher now answers the same three questions. QUILL watches several things for you — a watched folder, the weather, podcast feeds, a GitHub account — and each had grown its own habits: one had a poll interval, one had two, two had none. None could tell you it was still alive, and none let you decide whether a result should cut across what your screen reader was already saying. All four now expose the same policy triple: how often it checks, whether the check itself makes a sound, and whether a result interrupts speech. The tick is the one people miss until they have it — a short earcon on every check, so silence means “it stopped”, not “nothing new”. Both flags are off for every monitor by default, a zero-second interval is no longer representable (0 or a negative value is raised to the monitor’s floor on load and again on use), and QUILL reads the combination back as one sentence — “Checks every 15 minutes, ticks audibly, does not interrupt speech.” — rather than making you assemble it from three checkboxes. Shipping with it: a genuine background podcast new-episode check (opt-in, off in Safe Mode, reads feeds only), and the long-dead watch_folder_poll_interval_seconds setting finally drives the watched-folder pollers that were ignoring it. (core/monitor_policy.py, core/watch_profiles.py, core/watch_service.py, ui/podcasts/check_monitor.py)

  • QUILL gets an audio identity: every numbered slot has its own note, and progress is a sound, not a speech. Forty-eight new earcons, all designed and synthesized for the Ink pack (and overridable by any sound pack). Each of the twelve Copy Tray slots and ten quick-bookmark slots now plays its own pitch on a shared musical scale when you copy, paste, set, or jump — “slot seven” becomes a note you learn, so numbered features finally work at speed. Long downloads and installs play a short rising blip at every 5 percent (the quarter marks carry a touch of harmony, and 100 percent resolves), so progress never talks over your screen reader — the spoken 25/50/75 milestones stay as the coarse narration. Starting a selection with F8 and completing it play a mirrored two-note gate; the top and end of the document get a ceiling tick and a floor thud. And an opt-in Keep the sound device awake setting plays a silent clip every 20 seconds so USB and Bluetooth audio hardware never powers down and clips the first sound after a pause. Audition everything with python scripts/audition_ink_sounds.py; every sound has its own toggle in Sound Events. (quill/assets/sound_packs/ink/, scripts/gen_ink_sounds.py, core/sound_events.py, ui/sound_manager.py)

  • Suggest Document Metadata — the AI proposes, you approve, field by field. A new command (in the command palette: “Suggest Document Metadata…”) asks your configured AI for a title, summary, tags, and category for the current document, then opens a review dialog where each suggestion is judged on its own: you hear the field, what it currently holds, and what the AI proposes, and choose Accept, Accept and Next, Skip, or Copy. A field that already holds something always asks before being replaced — defaulting to No — and nothing touches the document until you choose Apply Accepted, which lands as a single undo step. This review-one-at-a-time pattern is the field counterpart of the word-level AI change review, and the dialog is reusable for any future feature that fills fields from AI output. (core/ai/field_apply.py, core/ai/metadata_suggest.py, ui/field_apply_dialog.py)

  • AI fallback offers now work in both directions — and an engine swap is never silent. When a cloud AI call fails from connectivity, QUILL already suggested your on-device model. Now the reverse works too: when the on-device model fails and you have a cloud provider configured with a key, QUILL says so — while making clear that switching sends your text to the cloud and that nothing switches until you choose it. And if QUILL ever has to start the chat on a different engine than the one you configured (your provider was unreachable at startup), it now says that the moment the chat opens instead of quietly answering with a different brain. (core/ai/fallback.py wiring, core/ai/assistant.py, ui/assistant_panel.py)

  • Errors keep getting more specific — phase two. Voice and component download failures, Quillin errors, and remote transport failures (SSH, FTP, S3, WebDAV) now carry their what-to-do sentence — “Check your internet connection, then retry the download from Help > Download Optional Components.” — through the same catalogue the AI dialogs adopted in phase one, which also grew eight new entries (Whisper model downloads, speech engine installs, microphone capture, unsupported save formats). A menu item disabled by a safety advisory now carries the reason in its help text, matching what the palette speaks. And the review rule is written down in CONTRIBUTING: a new user-visible error must name the specific condition and the next step. (core/error_codes.py and call sites)

  • If your screen reader stops, your work is already saved. QUILL now notices when the screen reader it detected at startup disappears mid-session. It waits for two missed checks — a routine JAWS or NVDA restart never triggers it — and then immediately snapshots every open document to autosave, announces what happened through whatever can still speak (another screen reader, or QUILL’s own built-in voice), and records it in Notifications so the explanation is waiting for you. QUILL keeps running: unlike tools that shut down when their screen reader dies, QUILL works with several readers and its own voice, so the right response is “your work is safe, here is what happened” — never a shutdown. When the reader comes back, QUILL says so. (core/sr_watchdog.py, ui/main_frame_sr_watchdog.py)

  • Paste any path and it just works. The Simple File Open dialog’s path field now accepts what people actually paste: a path copied from File Explorer with its quotes still on, %APPDATA%-style environment paths, file:// links from a browser, a leading ~, smart quotes, and the invisible non-breaking spaces web pages sneak in. The cleanup lives in one shared helper so every path field can adopt it. (core/path_input.py, ui/simple_open_dialog.py)

  • AI change review goes word-level, with the sentence around every change. The Review AI Changes dialog — the accept/reject checklist every AI edit passes through — used to describe changes by line, so a one-word edit in a 90-character line meant hearing the whole line twice and spotting the difference by ear. Each changed hunk now announces exactly what changed (“Changed ‘quick’ to ‘rapid’ at line 3”), adjacent edits merge into phrases (“quick brown” to “rapid red” is one change, not two), and the details pane shows the sentence before and after each change so a one-word edit can be judged the way a sighted reviewer judges highlighted text — with the full old and new lines still there below. Genuine rewrites deliberately fall back to line presentation (hearing forty word pairs is worse than hearing the lines whole), as do spacing-only edits. A pure model upgrade: the dialog, the agent editor, and the AI tool gateway all inherit it unchanged. (core/ai/diff_review.py)

  • Errors now tell you what to do next — the error-specificity programme begins. A message that names the exact failure is the difference between fixing it and filing a ticket, because a screen-reader user cannot glance at the failing file or dialog to see what went wrong. Three changes ship in this first pass. Coded errors can now carry a what-to-do sentence — “Install Pandoc from Help > Download Optional Components to convert this format.”, “Check the address, credentials, and connection under File > Manage Remote Sites.” — appended to the specific diagnosis, with the AI Q&A dialog and Ask Quill as the first adopters. The command palette now says why a command is unavailable (“unavailable: Turned off by a safety update: …”) on the row and when a run is refused, instead of a bare “(unavailable)”. And AI features no longer quietly answer over less than you sent: when a document is trimmed to fit the model, Ask Quill announces the working size (“The answer used the first 6,000 of the document’s 40,000 characters.”) and Document Q&A states how much of a large document its answer covers — and now speaks its status changes rather than only updating a label. (core/error_codes.py, core/commands.py, core/ai/assistant.py, core/ai/document_qa.py, ui/palette.py, ui/assistant_panel.py, ui/ai_document_qa_dialog.py)

  • Report Editor Surface — a spoken braille diagnostic in one keystroke. A new command (in the command palette; bindable in the Keymap Editor) speaks which editor surface is active, its native window class, whether system-edit emulation (the braille fix) is applied, whether the editor border is hidden, and whether braille output is live and through which backend. Nothing from the document is included. It is the spoken sibling of Copy Diagnostic Summary: when reporting a braille or speech problem, run it first and say what it said — a report from someone who cannot screenshot becomes actionable on the first message. (ui/main_frame_devtools.py)

  • Live spelling alerts get their own sound — and learn when to stay quiet. With spell check as you type on, finishing a misspelled word now plays a soft, distinct spelling earcon from the active sound pack instead of the bare system beep (the beep remains as a fallback when a pack has no spelling sound; toggle the sound in Sound Events). The alert is also suppressed inside web addresses, email addresses, Markdown inline code, and fenced code blocks — regions that are wall-to-wall false positives a sighted user filters visually. The F7 review still covers everything; only the ambient alert is filtered. (core/spellcheck.py, ui/main_frame_spellcheck.py, core/sound_events.py, assets/sound_packs/ink/)

  • The command palette understands what you mean — and teaches keystrokes to everyone. Multi-word searches now match in any order (url open and open url both find Open From URL…), a command’s shortcut text is searchable (ctrl+o finds Open), and common intent words work as aliases — settings finds Preferences, quit finds Exit, theme finds dark mode. And as you arrow through results the palette now speaks each command’s shortcut with its name, which the visual list always showed but screen-reader users never heard — so the palette teaches the faster route while it dispatches. (core/palette.py, ui/palette.py)

  • Insert Equation — write maths as text (#1197). Insert > Insert Equation… (Ctrl+Shift+E) inserts a LaTeX or MathML equation at the cursor: type E = mc^2 and choose Inline or Block, and QUILL adds the right delimiters ($...$ or $$ on their own lines). MathML goes in verbatim, since it already carries its own markup. Select an equation you already wrote — delimiters and all — and the shortcut reopens it for editing with the $ stripped off and the mode you used preselected. Typing maths as text is the accessible path: keyboard-only, reviewable character by character, and understood by screen readers that speak maths; the live preview and exported HTML render it, and the source stays plain text you can always arrow through. Contributed by @salorajan (split out of PR #1197). (ui/main_frame_equations.py)

  • Jump from the live preview to the editor (#1257). Reading the side-by-side preview and want to edit the paragraph you are on? Open the context menu on any block — heading, paragraph, list item, quote, table, or code block — with the Applications key, Shift+F10, or a right-click, and choose “Go to this location in the editor”: the caret moves to that block’s source, focus follows, and QUILL announces the landing. Enter does the same when your screen reader passes the key through, but the context menu is the contract, because browse mode gives no way to query where the virtual cursor is while a context menu always targets a concrete element. Under the hood the renderer stamps each block with its source line (only for the live preview — exports, the clipboard, publishing, and Vault previews still emit clean markup), and the jump is offered only when the preview matches the document verbatim, so positions always line up. (core/browser_preview.py, ui/preview_dialog.py, ui/main_frame_reveal_codes.py)

  • YouTube plays and records like any other station (#1268). Paste a YouTube link into Add Custom Station — a normal video link, a youtu.be short link, or a channel’s live page (youtube.com/@handle/live) — and it becomes a station: it plays through the same player, sits in Favorites, records with Record Now, and can be captured by a scheduled recording exactly like a radio stream. A link is saved as its tidy page address, never as a stream address, because YouTube’s stream addresses expire after a few hours; QUILL re-finds the audio every time the station plays or records, which is what lets a recording scheduled today still work next week. The lookup uses yt-dlp, which is never bundled: it installs on demand (about 3 MB) after a one-time consent and rights notice, shown when you add your first YouTube station — asked then, not at play time, so a scheduled recording firing while you are away is never the first time QUILL reaches YouTube. Unavailable in Safe Mode. Because finding the stream is a network round trip, it happens off the UI thread: you hear “Connecting” immediately and the window never freezes, and a lookup that lands after you pressed Stop or switched stations is discarded rather than hijacking playback. A private, removed, region-blocked, or not-yet-live video says so in plain words instead of failing silently. (core/radio/youtube.py, ui/radio/youtube_playback.py, ui/radio/youtube_ui.py, ui/radio/player_controller.py, core/radio/recording.py)

  • Quill Converter ships as its own app. The Universal Audio Converter is now a full standalone product alongside Quill Radio and Quill Cast, not only a window inside QUILL: standalone/converter/ carries the launcher, its own violet tile icon carrying the universal swap-arrows “convert” glyph (drawn in source, regenerable from assets/make_quill_converter_icon.py), the PyInstaller spec, and the project metadata, and the shared QuillVille builder gained a converter product that stages the bundled FFmpeg and nothing else — no speech engines, no playback stack, because the whole job is conversion. A portable QuillConverter.exe bundle keeps its settings, presets, and job history next to the executable like every sibling app. yt-dlp is still never bundled: URL import installs it on demand, with consent. (standalone/converter/*, standalone/studio/scripts/build_portable.py, core/storage_mode.py)

  • Paste a Live365 link and it just plays. Live365 hosts thousands of stations, but the link you actually have is almost never the stream — it is the station page (live365.com/station/...) or the web player (player.live365.com/a25891), both of which are web pages. Pasting one into Add Custom Station used to save a station that could never play, with nothing to explain why. QUILL now recognizes any Live365 station page, player link, or even a bare station id (a25891), rewrites it to that station’s real stream address, and says so in the dialog. It is a pure text rewrite — no network lookup, no scraping, nothing sent anywhere, nothing to gate in Safe Mode — and a URL that isn’t Live365 is passed through exactly as you typed it. (core/radio/live365.py, ui/radio/add_station_dialog.py)

  • The QuillVille menu — one standard cross-app switcher in every app. QUILL, Quill Radio, and Quill Weather each carry the same top-level QuillVille menu (Alt+Q), listing “Open QUILL / Quill Radio / Quill Weather / Quill Cast / Audio Studio” so you can jump to any family member — each opening in its own window — from the same place everywhere. Functional menus keep their descriptive names (the Weather menu stays “Weather”, which a screen reader can find by its first letter); QuillVille is the family-navigation menu, which is what a brand name should label. Quill Weather is a fully separate, independently-distributed app now (its own standalone/weather build folder, versioned 2.2.0 in lockstep with Quill Radio but updated on its own feed), and Quill Radio’s Weather menu also offers Open the Quill Weather App. Built on a small, tested launcher (core/app_launcher.py) that runs the sibling’s own exe in a frozen build or python -m quill.apps.<app> from source; because each app is single-instance, opening one that is already running just brings it forward. The app.open_radio / app.open_weather commands are also in QUILL’s command palette (rebindable). (core/app_launcher.py, ui/quillville_menu.py, ui/app_shell.py, apps/radio.py, apps/weather.py)

  • Show or hide any app with one global hotkey — each app its own unique chord. QUILL, Quill Radio, and Quill Weather each register a system-wide keystroke that tucks the window to the system tray, or brings it back and focuses it, even while another program has focus — so a family that lives in the tray (Quill Weather watching for alerts, Quill Radio playing in the background) is always one key away without hunting for the tray icon. The chords never collide: QUILL is Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Q, Quill Radio is Ctrl+Alt+Shift+R, Quill Weather is Ctrl+Alt+Shift+W. Hiding leaves the app fully running (playback, recording, and alert monitoring continue); the toggle speaks “hidden to the tray” or “shown” so a press from another window still tells you what happened. It is Windows-only (built on RegisterHotKey, the same mechanism Quill Radio already uses for the hardware media keys) and best-effort: if another program already owns a chord, that chord stays theirs and the app simply doesn’t grab it — nothing breaks, and QUILL’s press is a low-risk window-visibility command that can never touch a document. In QUILL the Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Q default is a normal, rebindable global hotkey you can change or clear in Tools > Global Hotkeys…. Built on a shared, unit-tested chord parser (ui/tray_hotkey.py). (ui/tray_hotkey.py, ui/app_shell.py, ui/main_frame_hotkeys.py, apps/radio.py, apps/weather.py)

  • Customize Features — turn whole areas of Quill Radio and Quill Weather on or off (like QUILL). A new View/Options > Customize Features… in the standalone apps lists each app’s switchable areas — Quill Radio’s Recording and Weather menus, Quill Weather’s Alert Monitoring and NOAA Weather Radio — each with a description; unchecking one omits its whole menu (and every command under it) the next time the app opens, so people can trim the apps to just what they use. Built on a shared, tested model and dialog (core/app_features.py, ui/app_features_dialog.py) reused by both apps, defaulting every area on so a new area is enabled until turned off. Richer tray menus too: Quill Radio’s tray gains Play/Pause and Stop; Quill Weather’s gains Quick Weather; both gain the sibling-app launchers. (core/app_features.py, ui/app_features_dialog.py, apps/radio.py, apps/weather.py, ui/main_frame_weather.py)

  • Calculator (Tools > Calculator) — a scientific calculator that also does the math in your document. A new accessible calculator that is two tools in one. It evaluates scientific and natural-language expressions safely — sqrt(264), sin(pi/6), 2^10 + 5!, 10 percent of 500, what is the square root of 264 — with no eval: it parses to an AST and walks an allowlist of operators, functions, and constants, so text pulled from a document can never execute code. And it does statistics over your data: select some numbers (or a column, or a pasted CSV or table) and it computes sum, average, minimum, maximum, count, product, median, range, or standard deviation — over all the numbers, down each column, or across each row, plus a one-line full summary — understanding currency symbols, thousands separators, percentages, and accounting negatives along the way. The input box is pre-filled with your editor selection (“select then calculate”), and any result can be copied or inserted at the cursor. This goes well beyond a standard scientific calculator with the column/row/table operations a calculator inside a writing tool should have. Core in the new quill/core/calc/ (evaluator.py, data_ops.py); dialog in quill/ui/calculator_dialog.py; opened from Tools > Calculator (rebindable as tools.calculator). (core/calc/*, ui/calculator_dialog.py, ui/main_frame_line_commands.py)

  • The Universal Audio Converter — convert audio (and pull audio out of video) between formats, offline, five ways. A complete audio converter that surfaces everywhere you’d reach for it: Audio Studio → Voices → Convert Audio…, a standalone Quill Converter app (python -m quill.apps.converter, its own tray-resident QuillVille window), a headless quill convert command for scripts, a right-click “Convert with Quill” entry in Windows Explorer, and Convert from URL… to pull a link’s audio down first. Build a mixed queue of files and whole folders, pick an output format (MP3, M4A/M4B, Opus, Ogg, FLAC, WAV, AAC, AIFF, ALAC, WMA, CAF — only the ones your ffmpeg can actually encode are offered) and a preset (MP3 128/192/320, Podcast, Audiobook, Voice memo, Web Opus, Archival FLAC, Hearing-aid mono, or Just convert), choose an output folder and a conflict policy (auto-number, skip, or overwrite), and it converts the whole batch across multiple workers off the UI thread with tray-aware progress and a spoken summary that always names any failures. Advanced mode adds a full DSP catalog — bit rate / sample rate / channels / bit depth, loudness-normalize (audiobook/podcast), gain, high-pass, trim silence, speed (tempo, no pitch shift), compressor, leveler, and fade in/out — each composed from QUILL’s already-tested filter builders. Video files (MP4, MKV, MOV, WebM, AVI…) have their audio track extracted automatically. Everything runs on your machine through the bundled ffmpeg (so it works in Safe Mode); only Convert from URL reaches the network, and only after a one-time consent + rights notice installs yt-dlp on demand (never bundled). The whole engine is wx-free and unit-tested (core/audio/convert.py, presets.py, dsp.py, url_import.py, convert_cli.py). (core/audio/*, ui/audio_studio/convert_audio_dialog.py, apps/converter.py, core/shell_verbs.py)

  • Quill Weather — a standalone tray app that watches for alerts even when everything else is closed (Quill Weather 2.2.0). The weather features below also ship as their own small app (quill-weather) whose whole job is to keep the National Weather Service alert watch running independently of Quill Radio or the editor. It launches to the system tray; closing its window minimizes to the tray so monitoring continues; and Options → Start Quill Weather with Windows (with Start minimized to the tray) makes it watch from login with no window ever shown. For a truly always-on watch with no process running at all, Options → Check for alerts in the background (even when Quill Weather is closed) registers a per-user Windows Scheduled Task that wakes a short-lived check on your monitor cadence, polls the NWS, and shows a Windows toast (which your screen reader announces) for any newly-issued alert, then exits — no NWWS credential or XMPP feed required. The live watch and the background check share one persisted “already-told-you” record so the same alert is never announced twice. (apps/weather.py, core/weather/headless_check.py, platform/windows/scheduled_task.py, platform/windows/weather_startup.py)

  • Weather Guardian — background alert monitoring with spoken warnings and a sounder (Quill Radio 2.2.0). Weather > Start/Stop Weather Monitoring (Ctrl+Shift+M) watches one US location’s active watches, warnings, and advisories on a timer and speaks each newly-issued alert as it appears — with forced, interrupting speech for tornado/flash-flood-level (Urgent and above) events — and announces when they all clear. It keeps running while the window is minimized to the tray, auto-resumes on launch, and shows a system-tray toast for each new alert. A severe-weather mode automatically tightens the poll (down to the NWS 30-second courtesy floor, default 60 s) while any alert is active, then relaxes back to the normal interval — near-real-time alerts on the free NWS API with no push feed. Weather > Pause/Resume Alert Checks temporarily snoozes the watch without turning it off (distinct from Stop, which is the full off). (core/weather/monitor.py, ui/main_frame_weather.py)

  • An alert sounder you control — on/off, your own sound, repeat count, and a Play preview (Quill Radio 2.2.0). New alerts play a distinctive bundled chime. In Weather > Settings you can turn the sound off entirely (alerts are still shown and spoken), choose your own .wav with a Play button to hear it before saving (and Use Default to go back), and set how many times it plays per alert (1–10). Best-effort and non-blocking, so a sound problem never delays the alert itself. (platform/alert_sound.py, assets/audio/weather_alert.wav, core/weather/settings.py, ui/weather/settings_dialog.py)

  • Test Alert — preview exactly what a real alert does (Quill Radio 2.2.0). Weather > Test Alert demonstrates the full experience in one click — the spoken/shown text, the sound (honoring your settings), a system-tray toast, and the alert dialog — all clearly marked as a TEST and dismissable, touching neither the network nor the monitor’s real state. So you can confirm what a warning will look and sound like before one ever arrives. (ui/main_frame_weather.py::weather_test_alert)

  • Hour-by-hour forecast (Quill Radio 2.2.0). The Weather Center gains an Hourly forecast pane — temperature, conditions, and chance of precipitation for each upcoming hour from the NWS hourly product — arrow through it like the daily outlook, with a configurable length (Settings › Hourly forecast (hours), default 24, 0 = off). (core/weather/nws.py::hourly_from_json, ui/weather/weather_center_dialog.py)

  • Moon almanac — phase, illumination, moonrise and moonset (Quill Radio 2.2.0). Current conditions and every day of the extended outlook now include the moon: its named phase, percent illuminated, and the day’s moonrise and moonset — computed locally with no extra network call or dependency (a compact lunar theory validated against known lunations). Toggle it in Settings › Moon phase, moonrise and moonset. (core/weather/astronomy.py)

  • Current local time at the searched location (Quill Radio 2.2.0). Weather Now and Quick Weather now lead with the location’s own local day and time (“The local time there is Thursday, April 27, 9:51 AM.”), so checking the weather in another city also tells you what time it is there — using the timezone the weather providers already return. Toggle in Settings › Current local time at the location. (core/weather/render.py::local_time_phrase)

  • Start Quill Radio with Windows (Quill Radio 2.2.0). A new Station > Start Quill Radio with Windows checkbox adds (or removes) a per-user autostart entry so Quill Radio launches at sign-in — the same reliable, no-elevation mechanism QUILL itself uses. (platform/windows/radio_startup.py, apps/radio.py)

  • Improve Reading Order with AI — fix a jumbled document in one command. A new AI > More > Improve Reading Order… (also on the command palette) sends the current document’s text to your configured AI provider to repair a scrambled reading order — a multi-column PDF, a page with sidebars or text boxes, or lines that came out of sequence — and opens the reflowed result as a new, unsaved document so your original is never touched. It reorders columns into a single natural flow, joins lines broken mid-sentence, and infers headings, lists, and tables, while preserving your wording exactly (it never summarizes or invents). It always asks first, naming the provider and its host and the approximate page size before anything is sent; it refuses documents over a configurable page limit (Settings › reading_order_max_pages, default 40) so a huge send can’t happen by accident; and it’s off in Safe Mode. It uses whichever AI backend you’ve configured (AI > Set Up AI) — OpenAI, Claude, Google Gemini, OpenRouter, Ollama (local), Ollama Cloud, or a custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint (bring-your-own-key; QUILL bundles none) — or, with no cloud provider set up, the bundled on-device model (Apple’s on-device model on a supported Mac, otherwise a local CPU model), so it can run entirely offline with nothing uploaded. Built on the assistant’s existing backend seam (Assistant.transform → make_default_backend), so it inherits every provider QUILL already supports. This closes the last editor-parity gap on the review list (“Improve Reading Order”). (core/ai/assistant.py reading_order operation, ui/main_frame_ai_reading_order.py, core/settings.py)

  • Open password-protected PDFs — QUILL asks for the password and reads the file (#58). Previously a locked PDF was correctly detected but refused: QUILL told you to remove the password yourself with an external tool (qpdf --decrypt) and open the decrypted copy. Now, when you open an encrypted PDF, QUILL prompts for its password and reads the document directly. Enter the password and the PDF opens; get it wrong and QUILL says so and lets you try again; press Cancel to abort the open. The password is used only to unlock that one file for reading and is never stored, logged, or written anywhere — reopen the file later and QUILL asks again. Both PDF extractors (pdfplumber and pypdf) receive the password through the read path, which now runs off the UI thread like every other office/PDF open, so the screen reader stays responsive while the file loads. Permissions-only “encrypted” PDFs (which open with an empty password) continue to open with no prompt, exactly as before. (io/pdf.py, io/structured.py, io/open_read.py, ui/main_frame.py::_ask_pdf_password)

  • Sort lines by date (Format > Sort Lines by Date). Alongside the existing alphabetical, numeric, and by-length line sorts, QUILL can now sort the selected lines (or the whole document) by the first date it finds on each line. It recognizes the common styles — ISO 2020-03-05, slash/dot forms like 03/04/2020 and 5.1.2020, and English month names (Jan 5, 2020, 5 January 2020, March 3rd, 1999) — and infers day/month order for an ambiguous numeric date (is 03/04 March 4th or 4 March?) from your region: US English reads month-first, everywhere else reads day-first, and an unambiguous component such as 25/12 always wins regardless. Two-digit years follow the standard pivot (00–68 → 2000s, 69–99 → 1900s). Lines with no recognizable date sink to the bottom in their original order, so nothing is lost. The reusable date parser lives in its own core/date_parse.py; the command is on the Format menu next to the other sorts and is rebindable in the Keymap Editor. (core/date_parse.py, core/format_ops.py::sort_lines_by_date, ui/main_frame_power_tools.py)

  • Hear the whole sentence around a misspelling — Ctrl+R in the Spelling Review. The F7 Spelling Review dialog gains a Read Sentence button and a Ctrl+R shortcut that reads the full sentence surrounding the current misspelled word aloud, so you can judge a correction in context without leaving the dialog or hunting through the document. It reads the same context the dialog already shows, whitespace-collapsed so it speaks smoothly. (ui/spelling_review_dialog.py, core/spelling/announcements.py::announce_context_sentence)

  • A PDF’s own bookmarks now open in the Bookmarks Manager. When you open a PDF that carries an embedded outline (Adobe bookmarks — its chapters and sections), QUILL now imports those entries into its Bookmarks Manager (Ctrl+Shift+G) automatically, the same way it already surfaces Word and EPUB structure — so you can jump straight to a chapter. Each imported bookmark lands exactly where Go To Page for that page would (resolved through the page markers QUILL writes when it extracts the PDF); the import runs once, on first open, and is skipped when the document already has bookmarks, so it never duplicates entries or resurrects ones you’ve since renamed or deleted; and it’s best-effort — a malformed or absent outline never blocks the open. (io/pdf.py::extract_pdf_outline, io/structured.py, ui/main_frame.py::_import_pdf_outline_bookmarks)

  • Six more one-click writing presets in the Prompt Library. The built-in Prompt Library gains Generate FAQs, Draft a Speech, Summary Email, Social Media Post (≤280 characters), Step-by-Step Instructions, and Paraphrase — the named one-shot tools QUILL was missing next to its existing summarize / rewrite / tone / expand / extract-action-items set. Each runs over your selection or the whole document from AI > Prompt Library, and (like every built-in) its wording can be tweaked and it can be disabled but not deleted. (core/prompt_library.py)

  • Cross-format conversion keeps far more of your formatting — with a documented fidelity matrix. A round of work to make Save As / conversion between Markdown, HTML, Word, RTF, and plain text preserve as much as possible: tables now convert as real tables between Markdown, HTML, and Word (a Markdown/HTML table saved to Word becomes a genuine, editable Word table with a bold, repeating header row that screen readers announce — not pipe-text; Word tables already came across as native Markdown/HTML tables); docx hyperlinks now round-trip both ways (a real <w:hyperlink> is written and its target is read back, instead of links degrading to plain text); numbered lists are first-class across Markdown, HTML, and Word (a 1. 2. 3. list stays an ordered list, keeping a non-1 start); and Markdown → HTML now renders images as <img> (previously a broken ! plus a link) and blockquotes as <blockquote> (previously a literal >). A new comprehensive test suite (tests/unit/io/test_save_as_format_fidelity.py) verifies headings, bold, italic, underline, strike, links, bullet + numbered lists, tables, images, colour, and more across every direction, and the honest limits are now documented in the User Guide (“What carries over between formats”) and the PRD: blockquote/code/rule are literal markers in Word/RTF; a table saved to RTF is readable pipe-text rather than a native RTF table; inline formatting inside a Markdown-authored table cell flattens to plain text in Word; Word-embedded images are not extracted; RTF drops alignment and page breaks. (io/rtf_model.py, io/docx_reader.py, io/docx_writer.py, core/browser_preview.py)

  • Quill Radio multi-window model — the menu bar never disappears (Quill Radio 2.2.0). Browse Stations, Search Stations, Manage Favorites, Schedule Recording, and the Weather Center become modeless wx.Frames that each carry the shared menu bar (fixing a reported “menus vanish” defect — a wx.Dialog cannot host a menu bar) and no longer lock out the main window. A new &Window menu lists open windows numbered 1–9; Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab cycle them and Ctrl+1–Ctrl+9 jump to one, with “Entered …”/“Exited …” cues and primary-control focus on entry. Built on a pure, unit-tested WindowRegistry plus a thin wx WindowManager; each surface takes an optional windows= param so the same class is a modeless frame in standalone Radio and the classic modal dialog when embedded in QUILL. (ui/window_manager.py, ui/window_menu.py, radio surfaces)

  • Back up and restore your Radio stations and settings (Quill Radio 2.2.0, #1193). New Station > Back Up Stations and Settings… and Restore from Backup… save favorites, settings, the wake timer, and the recording schedule – and, on request, the recorded audio – into one portable .qrbackup zip, and bring them back on a new device or after a reinstall. File-level so a backup made by one version restores into another; the restore validates the manifest, accepts only the known state filenames, is zip-slip-guarded, and reloads the app live. (core/radio/backup.py, ui/radio/backup_ui.py)

  • Ctrl+T opens a reviewable Now Playing window (Quill Radio 2.2.0). “What’s Playing?” now opens the NowPlayingDialog – the current title and artist in a read-only, arrow-through, copyable field – instead of only speaking the title; it falls back to fetch-and-speak when no title is cached yet. The window (#1134) existed but had no menu item or key, so nothing opened it. (apps/radio.py, ui/main_frame_radio.py)

  • Station Details for any favorite (Quill Radio 2.2.0). A favorite’s context menu gains Station Details…, opening the same reviewable, copyable readout (source, stream, format, country) the search results show. (apps/radio.py)

  • Radio Browser (by Genre) in Browse Stations (Quill Radio 2.2.0, #1194). A first-class Browse-tree branch to walk the Radio Browser directory by genre, not only search it (thin adapters over the existing tag/search API); and the Search source filter now reads “Radio Browser” (two words) so a screen reader does not make the run-together label sound missing. (core/radio/radio_browser.py, ui/radio/station_browser_dialog.py, ui/radio/browse_tree_dialog.py)

  • NOAA Weather Radio is now an authoritative directory (Quill Radio 2.1.1). The Browse Stations “Weather / NOAA” branch drops the fuzzy RadioBrowser name search for the WeatherIndex directory (api.wxindex.org) of actual NWR transmitters: browse state by state (call sign, frequency, place), search by SAME code, call sign, or “County, ST”, and play the best available re-stream. A three-tier resolver – live API, app-data cache, bundled snapshot of 1,035 transmitters (quill/data/noaa_directory.json) – keeps every NOAA feature working fully offline. Weather menu > Listen to your Local NOAA Weather Radio resolves the transmitter covering your saved Weather location (county/SAME first, nearest covering transmitter by coordinates as fallback); Weather menu > Update NOAA Weather Radio Directory pulls the newest directory on demand into the cache tier, never overwriting the bundled floor. Live pulls are Safe-Mode-blocked and in the network-egress audit. (core/radio/wxindex*.py, scripts/snapshot_wxindex.py)

  • Radio Reading Services (Quill Radio 2.1.1). A new “Radio Reading Services” Browse category and search blend for the audio information services that read newspapers and local print aloud for blind and print-disabled listeners – WRBH, Sun Sounds of Arizona, CRIS, KPBS/WKAR reading services, ACB Media 1-5, NFB Radio Network, Voice Corps, and more. 20 vetted services ship as a bundled snapshot (quill/data/reading_services.json) so the category works offline; Station > Update Radio Reading Services refreshes live from RadioBrowser (cache -> live -> snapshot resolver mirroring wxindex, Safe-Mode-guarded). Stations play, favorite, record, and schedule like any other. (core/radio/reading_services.py)

  • “Find in this folder” search in Browse Stations. A search box in the Browse tree that searches from the currently-highlighted folder downward only – one genre, one state, one source – instead of the whole app, so results stay small and relevant. It loads that folder’s subtree (bounded by depth, a result cap, and a fetch budget so a search from a big branch or TuneIn’s remote tree stays finite), filters its stations by name, and shows the matches as a flat list under the folder. Clear drops the results and returns the cursor to the folder you searched from. Works for every source – favorites, station lists, genre catalogs, NOAA states, iHeart genres, TuneIn folders. (ui/radio/browse_tree_dialog.py)

  • iHeart is now in Browse Stations, not just Search. A new iHeart branch in the Browse Stations tree groups iHeart’s live stations by genre, and within each genre by an A-Z sub-directory (with 0-9 and # buckets), every level loading lazily. It uses iHeart’s free, keyless JSON content API (us.api.iheart.com) because the XML sitemap the Search path uses carries no genre; each station row already embeds its stream, so a genre resolves in one request with no per-station page fetch. Leaves play, favorite, record, and schedule like any other station. Reached only by expanding the branch, and off in Safe Mode; the egress runs through the existing reviewed iheart._fetch site. (core/radio/iheart.py, ui/radio/browse_tree_dialog.py)

  • Format-aware Writing Tools: Block Quote, Horizontal Rule, and a smarter Insert Image. The Insert menu gains Insert Block Quote and Insert Horizontal Rule, and every structured insert (block quote, horizontal rule, table, image) is now format-aware: it emits Markdown in a Markdown document and HTML in an HTML document. When the document’s format isn’t set yet (a fresh, unsaved, or plain buffer), QUILL asks “Markdown or HTML?” once, then remembers the choice for that document and stops asking — so inserts just work without turning a plain buffer into a guessing game. The structured inserts now carry direct authoring chords that mirror established authoring muscle memory: Insert Table = Ctrl+Alt+T, Insert Block Quote = Ctrl+Alt+Q, Insert Horizontal Rule = Ctrl+Alt+H (joining the existing Ctrl+Alt+1–6 headings and Ctrl+Alt+7/8 lists). None of the three collides with a default NVDA/JAWS command, and all remain rebindable in the Keymap Editor. (core/keymap.py, core/tagging.py, ui/main_frame.py::_resolve_structured_markup, ui/main_frame_commands.py, ui/main_frame_menu.py, tools/menu_lint.py)

  • Insert Image now drafts alt text with AI and lays out correctly in HTML. The Insert Image dialog gains two enrichments on top of its mandatory-alt-text promise. When an AI vision model is connected (and you are not in Safe Mode), a Suggest alt text with AI button describes the chosen file and drops the draft into the alt field for you to check and edit — the machine drafts, you approve; the vision call runs off the UI thread behind a progress indicator and, unlike the older describe path, is correctly disabled in Safe Mode. In an HTML document the dialog also collects width/height (so the browser reserves the box and the page doesn’t jump as the image loads), a Responsive cap (max-width:100%, never overflowing a column), and an optional Caption (emitted as <figure>/<figcaption> so the caption is programmatically tied to the image); decorative images emit empty alt plus role="presentation". These fields are hidden in Markdown, whose syntax cannot carry them. (core/inline_image_alt.py::build_image_html, ui/insert_image_dialog.py, ui/main_frame_image_alt.py)

  • ePub heading navigation reaches every chapter heading — even inferred ones. ePub chapters now render their in-chapter headings inline (as Markdown # lines two levels below the chapter), so single-key heading navigation (H / Shift+H) walks them instead of stopping at chapter boundaries. And when a chapter has no real <h1>–<h6> markup at all (common in older, hand-made EPUBs), QUILL now infers headings from structure — a paragraph whose class names it a title/chapter/section, or a short standalone bold line acting as a heading — so navigation and the chapter outline still work where they used to come up empty. Long bold runs (emphasis inside a paragraph) are deliberately left alone. (core/epub.py)

  • Bookmarks that survive edits — “Points” you can trust. Bookmarks (and the numbered quick-bookmarks) no longer store just a raw character position that silently drifts the moment you insert or delete text above them. Each now also captures the text around it, so jumping to a bookmark re-anchors to where that text actually lives now — text added or removed above the mark no longer lands you in the wrong place. If the bookmarked text was itself deleted, the jump falls back to the old clamped position (never worse than before). Anchors persist per-document across restarts and are fully backward-compatible with existing bookmark files. (core/bookmark_anchor.py, core/bookmarks.py, ui/main_frame.py)

  • Word tables are no longer lost when editing a .docx as a rich document. Opening a Word document for rich editing used to drop every table silently — the cell contents simply vanished. Tables now appear inline, in reading order, rendered as accessible Markdown tables, so their content is preserved, readable, and reachable by single-key table navigation (T). (The classic open path already showed Word tables via MarkItDown; this closes the gap on the rich-edit path. scan_docx_features still flags tables honestly before a rich edit.) (io/docx_reader.py)

New

  • QUILL’s own Find and Replace dialog (optional, #1327 phase 1). Turn on Settings > Use QUILL’s own Find dialog and Ctrl+F / Ctrl+H open an accessible Find/Replace built for screen-reader users instead of the native Windows one: an Extended mode that searches for characters you cannot type (\n, \t, \xNN, \N{EM DASH}, plus an Insert Special Character picker of forty named characters — non-breaking space, curly quotes, zero-width space…), a spoken match count as you type, peek navigation (Ctrl+Up/Down walk the matches while focus stays in the search field), every match announced as the sentence around it, wrap-around announced when it happens, a Count button, replacements applied as range edits so undo works normally, and — closing the report that started the project — a Direction radio group whose name is actually announced. Ctrl+F refocuses the open dialog, Ctrl+H switches it to Replace in place, Ctrl+L returns to the search field from anywhere. Ships OFF by default until the JAWS/NVDA validation pass; the native dialog remains the default and stays available afterward. All match logic is a wx-free, unit-tested core module. (quill/core/find_model.py, quill/ui/find_dialog.py)
  • Regular Expression Helper 2.0 (#1328 phase 1). The helper grows from six recipes in a flat list to a category tree of 100+ recipes across cleanup, words, lines, numbers, dates, contact and web, Markdown, HTML, punctuation, writing checks, OCR scan cleanup, code identifiers, and capture-and-replace transformations with ready replace templates — and gains a plain-language explain engine: any pattern, typed or chosen, is narrated step by step (“One or more digits. Optionally: a period…”) and a broken pattern is diagnosed in words at the exact character position. Previews read every match as a sentence with line and column, show replace previews for transformation recipes, and Use in Find All Matches sends the pattern straight into the search flow with regex mode on — no clipboard hop. The catalog, explain engine, and bounded-execution runner are wx-free, strict-typed core modules with exhaustive tests, and the dialog itself moved out of the main-frame monolith. (quill/core/regex_helper/, quill/ui/regex_helper_dialog.py)
  • Paste Markdown as HTML (Edit menu). The mirror of Paste HTML as Markdown: Markdown on the clipboard is rendered to a clean HTML body fragment — through the same renderer as the live preview and HTML export — and inserted at the cursor, honoring the read-only guard. Unbound by default (the QUILL-key letter space is fully assigned); rebindable in the Keymap Editor and in the command palette as “Paste Markdown as HTML”. (quill/ui/main_frame_power_tools.py)
  • AI writing on the on-device model reads cleaner, and local summaries stay closer to the source (#1319, #1320). When your AI runs on the bundled on-device model — the free, offline, nothing-leaves-your-machine path — QUILL’s generative writing tools (Rewrite, Summarize, Expand, Continue, Shorten) now shape the output the way a good editor would, because small local models have consistent, catchable tics. Each of those prompts is primed with a few wrong-then-corrected worked examples (on small models, examples beat instructions), and QUILL then runs a deterministic pass over what the model wrote that catches hedging (“it seems”, “arguably”), editorializing (“clearly”, “obviously”), and filler openers (“In today’s world”, “It is important to note”) and does one cheap retry to remove them — no second model call is needed to judge, so it stays fast. Faithful transforms (Fix Grammar, Improve Reading Order) are deliberately excluded, so a “banned” word that is genuinely in your source is never rewritten. Separately, Summarize on the on-device model now runs in two passes — pass one extracts the plain observations from your text, pass two rewrites them into a word-budgeted summary with the source out of view — which sharply cuts the invented interpretation small models otherwise add. Both behaviours are on automatically for the on-device model only; every cloud provider is untouched and answers exactly as before. (quill/core/ai/quality_filter.py, quill/core/ai/two_pass.py, quill/core/ai/assistant.py)
  • Foundations for longer, safer AI jobs — three tested core modules, not yet surfaced (#1321, #1322, #1323). Three pieces of groundwork land in this release as fully unit-tested core modules with assistant seams, but are not yet wired into any screen or command — creating a context sidecar or running a batch job does not surface them yet, and adoption comes in a later release. Focus-free progress + ETA (quill/stability/progress.py): a pure reporter a long job (bulk transcription, agent runs, OCR) can drive to post its progress to the window title and a UIA live-region status line — pollable on your own schedule with nothing stealing focus — with the ETA computed from the second unit to skip warm-up skew and an up-front heads-up when a run will take hours. A .quill-context.md sidecar (quill/core/ai/sidecar_context.py): a resolver for a plain-Markdown reference file beside a document (character names, terminology, project facts) that would be injected as reference context and announced when used — the facts companion to the existing .quill-instructions.md writing-rules sidecar; the assistant seam (set_context_preamble) exists, but the document surfaces do not call it yet. Crash-safe batch records (quill/core/ai/resume_record.py): atomic-JSON records that let an interrupted batch AI run resume where it stopped and rebuild its outputs with zero model calls — interruption safety for the slow, CPU-only machines that are QUILL’s core audience. (quill/stability/progress.py, quill/core/ai/sidecar_context.py, quill/core/ai/resume_record.py)

Fixes

  • Stations on SecureNet’s player now resolve — Find Streams stopped throwing away the one URL that worked. Paste a securenetsystems.net/v5/… player page into Add Custom Station and, until now, you got either a couple of useless links or nothing at all. The stream was never missing: it is a plain string sitting in the page. QUILL discarded it because it looks like an ordinary link — https://ice66.securenetsystems.net/ROM has no .mp3 on the end and no /stream in the path, which is all the generic scan had to go on when deciding which of a page’s links might be audio. SecureNet hosts the player for a large number of US broadcasters, so this was never about two stations; it just took two of them (Radio Once More and Radio Once More 2, reported 2026-08-07) to surface it. QUILL now recognises the player itself — by address, or by the mount in the page when a station embeds the player on its own site — and offers the real stream first, ahead of the merely stream-shaped links. Per-visit tracking ids are stripped so a saved favourite stays good, the station is labelled with its true callsign whatever the casing of the link you pasted, and a page that genuinely carries no stream still says so rather than offering a URL that will not play. Self-healing repair picks it up too: a station saved from one of these player pages now fixes itself on the first failed play, where before the page’s own chatty links made the result look ambiguous and the repair declined to act. No extra network request: it reads the page the scan already fetched, so it works in Safe Mode too. Ported to the macOS build of Quill Radio at the same time. (core/radio/securenet.py, core/radio/link_finder.py, core/radio/recovery.py)

  • Temporary bookmarks are back — on Ctrl+J and Ctrl+Shift+J (#1317). The one-shot unnamed jump point from 0.9.0 Beta 3 — set a point, wander, snap straight back, no dialog and no name — silently disappeared when its original chords (Ctrl+Shift+K / Alt+Shift+K) were reassigned to Unquote Lines and Keep Unique Lines: the commands were dropped rather than rebound, so no keystroke, menu item, or palette entry could reach them. They are re-registered as Set Temporary Bookmark (Ctrl+J) and Go to Temporary Bookmark (Ctrl+Shift+J), with Navigate-menu items, command-palette entries, F1 help topics, and rebindable ids in the Keymap Editor. Behavior is exactly as before: setting a new temporary bookmark silently replaces the old one, and it never persists between sessions — disposable scratch state by design. (ui/main_frame.py, core/keymap.py, ui/main_frame_menu.py)

  • Repeat Last Announcement and the Announcement Self-Test now actually exist in the companion apps. Both commands were written to be shared by every app, but the code that registered them was never called — so in Quill Radio, QUILL Cast, Quill Weather, Quill Converter, Audio Studio and QuillBeacon they were dead code, even though the documentation said they shipped everywhere. They are registered now, and reachable from each app’s command palette. (ui/app_shell.py)

  • Installing a companion app could have fetched the wrong one. The release-asset matching used a simple name prefix, and QUILL’s own prefix is the start of every companion’s — so asking for QUILL from inside Quill Radio could have downloaded Radio’s own package. Audio Studio was worse off: its prefix matched neither of the two file names its build actually publishes, so its download would never have resolved at all once it ships. Matching now takes the most specific app name, and both cases are covered by tests. (core/companion_install.py)

  • Table cell navigation now holds its shape on tables imported from Word — and tells you when the table ends, not just when the row does. Word tables are brought into QUILL as Markdown rows, so Ctrl+Alt+arrow cell movement has always worked on them; what did not work was a cell whose own text contained a |. That character split the row, so a three-column table announced itself as “column 2 of 4”, the header stopped lining up with the body, and moving down a column landed in the wrong place. Pipes inside a cell are now escaped on the way in and treated as text on the way out, by both Word readers, so the grid keeps the column count the document actually has and the cell is spoken with its pipe intact. Two related repairs ride along: the RTF reader was silently discarding every literal backslash and brace, which ate that escape on the round trip that rich Word editing takes; and the edge announcements now distinguish the end of a row from the end of the table — “No more cells, end of table” rather than the same “No more cells” you hear four rows earlier. (core/table_nav.py, io/docx_text.py, io/rtf.py)

  • Mastodon bios and posts are no longer read out as raw web markup. Everything a Mastodon server sends as human text — a post’s content, an account’s bio, even a display name — arrives as HTML, and QUILL had no conversion step anywhere, so the markup went straight to the display and the screen reader. Bios and posts are now flattened to plain text at the single point where the server’s reply becomes something QUILL shows or speaks, so no surface can leak tags again; a link is heard as the part your server chose to display rather than the long hidden URL behind it. Three transformations that exist only because this text is spoken ride along: a reply opening with a wall of @mentions is condensed to “@alice and 2 more” so you reach the first real word immediately, emoji and decorative characters are stripped from names and posts (accented letters, other alphabets and ordinary punctuation are kept, and an all-emoji name falls back to the handle), and each post is summarised as “3 minutes ago: ” — a spoken time instead of a timestamp, and one post per key press when you arrow the list. View Mastodon Profile… now also shows the account’s bio and recent posts. (core/speech_text.py, core/mastodon/social.py, ui/mastodon_social_dialogs.py)

  • Every destructive question now defaults to No — across the whole family, and enforced forever. Twenty-seven confirmations in QUILL, Quill Radio, QUILL Cast, and QuillBeacon — “Delete this recording?”, “Clear all slots?”, “Remove this component?” — had Yes as their default button, so pressing Enter reflexively destroyed the thing. All of them now default to No: Enter is always the safe answer, and you choose Yes deliberately. A new automated check in the build fails if anyone ever adds a destructive Yes-default again, so this stays fixed. (quill/tools/dialog_button_contract.py and 17 dialog files)

  • A burst of braille messages no longer flickers across the display. QUILL already skipped an identical message repeated within two seconds, but a burst of different messages — a status cascade, a fast poll — still flashed one on top of the next, faster than cell one could be read. The first message of a quiet period is still written instantly; anything landing within the next 150 ms now settles to the newest message instead of each one shoving the last aside. Errors are exempt and always write through at once. (ui/announce_wiring.py)

  • Copy Tray slot 10 gains a menu access key, and the docs now say twelve. The Copy Tray menu’s slot 10 items use the standard 1&0 mnemonic (slots 11 and 12 are reachable by arrowing, the paste chords, and the dialog — a &10 label would have collided with slot 1), and two stale docstrings still describing a “nine-slot” tray were corrected to the twelve slots the tray has actually had. (ui/main_frame_menu.py, ui/main_frame_copy_tray.py, core/copy_tray.py)

  • Quill Cast starts again. The app died before its window ever appeared: its shows tree hides its root (so the top level reads as folders and shows rather than a pointless “root” node), and wxWidgets refuses to expand a hidden root – Can't expand/collapse hidden root node! – which took the whole app down. The call was never doing anything in the first place: a hidden root is always effectively expanded, because its children are the visible top level. Found by launching all six companion apps as part of the announcement work, which is exactly what a smoke pass is for. (apps/podcasts.py)

  • Every app announces properly now, and QuillBeacon speaks at all (#1283). The announcement work that started with braille became a service every shell shares, and the results are uneven only because the starting points were: QuillBeacon had no screen-reader speech whatsoever — its announcer wrote the status bar or, failing that, the window title, and screen readers announce neither, so “ready”, result counts, filter changes and undo confirmations were all silent. Quill Radio, Cast, Audio Studio, Converter, Weather and Podcasts had speech but no braille, no earcons, and no Quiet Mode. All of them now get speech, braille, earcons, the status line and Quiet Mode from one place. (Notification history and the spoken-echo buffer remain QUILL-only: those two channels need a notification store the companion shells do not have yet.) Along with it: Repeat Last Announcement (speech is gone the moment it finishes — this brings it back) and an Announcement Self-Test that announces a phrase and then tells you which channels actually delivered and which backend served each, so “braille is broken” and “no display is connected” stop looking the same. Fourteen new sound cues cover the companion apps, all disableable and pack-overridable. New accessibility settings control braille style (including the compact p7/87 l14 c3 form), the braille repeat window, sticky errors, app sounds, cue-instead-of-speech in Quiet Mode, and which severities interrupt. (core/announce/*, ui/announce_wiring.py, ui/announce_commands.py, apps/beacon/announce.py)

  • Announcements now reach your braille display (#1283). A braille user reported that status and informational messages QUILL speaks never appear in braille — What’s Playing (Ctrl+T), a finished weather refresh, a Radio Reading Services update. The diagnosis was blunter than the report: QUILL had never sent anything to a braille display at all. Every announcement path spoke; not one called either bridge’s braille API, even though both already support it — Prism through Backend.braille(), accessible_output2 through JAWS’s BrailleString and NVDA’s nvdaController_brailleMessage. Braille is now routed at the same choke point as speech, so every message in QUILL, Quill Radio, and Quill Cast is fixed at once. Three rules govern it: braille never costs speech (an unplugged display, a reader that rejects the call, a bridge without the capability — all degrade to “spoke but did not braille”, never to silence); nothing is truncated (both readers let you pan a long flash message, and clipping would drop the end of a track title); and an identical message inside two seconds does not steal the display twice, because a flash replaces whatever is under your fingers. Turn it off with Show announcements in braille in Preferences > Accessibility. This also finally connects the verbosity system’s braille channel, which had been rendered and discarded since it was built. (platform/windows/braille_output.py, platform/windows/prism_bridge.py, core/verbosity/controller.py)

  • Quill Radio remembers your volume, and Ctrl+Up/Down works from anywhere (#1263). The player started every session at 100% unless the station was a favorite with its own remembered level — so a non-favorite station came back at full blast on the next launch, which one listener described as loud enough to hurt. The last level you set is now saved and restored (a favorite’s own level still wins), and the save is disk-only so setting the volume no longer reloads the favorites tree or re-announces the station. Separately, Ctrl+Up and Ctrl+Down only adjusted the volume while the favorites tree had focus, because everywhere else they relied on a bare Ctrl+arrow menu accelerator, which Win32 does not deliver reliably; they are now handled in the frame’s key hook, so they work from any focus — except inside a text field, where Ctrl+arrow still edits text. (core/radio/history.py, ui/main_frame_radio.py, apps/radio.py)

  • Reveal Codes no longer speaks every code twice (#1244, #1245). Arrowing through the flowed Reveal Codes pane spoke each code twice under NVDA: the pane is a live text control, so the screen reader narrates the caret move itself, and QUILL also spoke its own rich phrase (“italic on, 15 characters”) on top of it. Because JAWS and NVDA interleave their reading with ours differently, the two screen readers also reported inconsistently. Arrow navigation now mirrors that phrase to the status bar silently — exactly as structured mode already did — so the screen reader is the single voice: JAWS’s “1 of 4” and NVDA’s line read both come through cleanly, and the character count is still there to review on the status bar. Explicit jumps (next/previous code, go to pair) and mouse clicks still speak, since those have no caret narration to collide with. If you preferred the spoken examination, the new Reveal Codes: Speak Codes Aloud command (setting reveal_codes_speak, off by default) brings it back. (ui/reveal_codes_pane.py, ui/main_frame_reveal_codes.py, core/settings.py)

  • “Copy What’s Playing” and “What’s Playing - Review and Copy” now always answer you (#1282). With a station playing, both commands could return to the main window having done nothing at all — no window, no copy, no message — while with nothing playing they spoke a sensible message, which made the bug look inverted. Three faults: a missing track title was treated as “nothing is playing” (normal for the first seconds of a station, for a stream whose ICY tap is refused, and for every station when track announcements are off); the fallback was an asynchronous fetch whose failure path was silently swallowed; and in the standalone apps a successful copy reported failure, because AppShellFrame._copy_to_clipboard returned None where MainFrame’s returns a bool and the shared radio code branches on it. Now: if a station is on, both commands fetch the title first (“Checking what’s playing…”), then copy it or open the review window; a stream with no titles says so and still opens a window naming the station; a failed fetch is reported instead of swallowed; and the copy confirmation names what it copied. (ui/radio/now_playing_commands.py, ui/main_frame_radio.py, ui/app_shell.py)

  • Opened documents no longer start with text QUILL added (#1279). A Word document opened with # DOCX Extract sitting above its first line, and PDFs opened with a four-line # PDF Extract / Engine: / Quality score: banner – text that is not in your file, read out first by a screen reader every time, and (for PDFs) fed to the AI along with the document. The built-in extract for Word, ODT, PowerPoint, Excel, and PDF now contains the document’s own text only. Nothing was lost: the format, engine, page counts, quality score, and the low-confidence “OCR may read this better” advice all live in Document Intake Report, and the open announcement still states them (“Opened report.pdf. 12 pages. extracted by pdfplumber. quality 84/100.”). Sheet and slide headings stay – those are the file’s own structure. (io/structured.py, io/pdf.py::format_pdf_document, core/intake.py)

  • A JSON file that starts with a byte order mark opens (#1278). Opening a UTF-8-with-BOM .json crashed with JSONDecodeError: Unexpected UTF-8 BOM (0.9.0) and, after the malformed-file guard, opened as unformatted raw text. Structured opens now go through the same reader plain text has used since #648: the BOM is stripped from the editable text and remembered, so the file parses and pretty-prints normally and a save writes the BOM back byte-for-byte. .xml, .toml, .yaml, and .ipynb get the same treatment, and these files now also keep their original CRLF line endings and fall back to cp1252/latin-1 for non-UTF-8 bytes instead of failing. (io/structured.py::read_structured_document)

  • Save As to Markdown/HTML from a Word document keeps its formatting. Opening a .docx in rich mode makes document.text a flattened plain-text mirror, and Save As to .md/.html serialized that — so headings, lists, bold, italic, and links were all lost (Save As to .rtf/.docx was already fine, and documents opened on the extract floor were fine, which made the loss inconsistent and easy to miss). Rich-mode Save As now reconstructs the RichDocument from the control’s RTF and renders it with rich_to_markdown (Markdown) or markdown_to_html (HTML), so the formatting comes across as native markup. (Underline has no standard Markdown form, so it round-trips as QUILL’s hidden-codes span, and renders natively in HTML.) The Save As dialog and its format routing were correct — this was a missing converter wiring in the rich-mode save path. (ui/main_frame_rich_mode.py::_save_rich_as_markup)

  • Podcast search returns you to the results list after subscribing (#1181 follow-up). In Add Podcast…, subscribing to an iTunes search result left focus stranded on the Subscribe button, so a screen-reader user had to hunt back to the list to add the next show. Focus now returns to the results list and re-selects the row you subscribed from (clamped to the current list), ready to arrow on. The Add-by-Feed-URL path is unchanged (focus stays by the URL box). Applies on success, already-subscribed, and error outcomes. (ui/podcasts/add_podcast_dialog.py::_return_focus_to_results)

  • The accessible Word view no longer crashes on the first edit (#1198). Typing into the Word structure surface raised TypeError: MainFrame._sync_editor_change() missing 1 required positional argument: 'status', because WordDocumentSurface invokes its on_change callback with no arguments while _sync_editor_change required a status. The status now defaults to "Modified" (matching the plain-editor callers), so the surface’s zero-argument callback works, and a signature contract test guards both halves so the arity can’t drift apart again. (ui/main_frame.py::_sync_editor_change)

  • A single non-UTF-8 file no longer takes down a whole Vault scan (#1202). Opening a folder as a Vault crashed with UnicodeDecodeError if any note inside was not valid UTF-8 (for example a Latin-1 file, or a stray non-text file in the folder). scan_vault now re-reads such a file leniently (errors="replace") so the note is still indexed and searchable instead of aborting the scan. (core/vault/vault.py::scan_vault)

  • Launching Quill Radio no longer crashes on a stray keystroke (#1203). A global key hook could fire before (or after) a document editor existed, and _focus_is_in_document_surface read self.editor/self.notebook directly, raising AttributeError: 'MainFrame' object has no attribute 'editor' — most visibly while starting Quill Radio, which has no editor. All three surface checks are getattr-guarded (the same hardening as #951) and covered by a regression test. (ui/main_frame.py::_focus_is_in_document_surface)

  • The live preview no longer renumbers ordered lists (#1200). A numbered list that started at 3 (or used any non-1 start) showed correctly in the editor but was silently renumbered to start at 1 in the preview, and a loose list (blank lines between items) split into separate lists that each restarted. The Markdown preview now emits <ol start="N"> to honor the author’s starting number and keeps a blank-line-separated list as one list, so the preview matches what you typed. (core/browser_preview.py::_render_markdown)

  • Custom radio stations can be added to Favorites (#1205). Adding a custom station played it but the favorites list never visibly updated — the station was saved but the tree wasn’t refreshed, so it looked like nothing happened. The Add Custom Station flow now refreshes the favorites tree and the favorite toggle immediately (guarded so it stays a no-op in embedded QUILL, which has no favorites tree). (ui/main_frame_radio.py::_radio_open_add_custom)

  • Browse Stations picks up new listings after an in-place update (#1207). After updating in place, the app-data directory cache written by the previous version — still inside its 7-day freshness window — kept shadowing the newer bundled station directory, so Browse Stations showed the old listings even though the app had updated. The cache is now bypassed when the bundled snapshot is newer than it (a genuinely fresher user-refreshed cache still wins), so new listings appear without waiting out the age window. (core/radio/wxindex.py::_directory_stations)

  • Alt+S presses Stop instead of opening the Stations menu (#1208). The transport button’s &Stop/&Play label advertised Alt+S / Alt+P as its access key, but those collide with the Station and Playback menu-bar mnemonics (the menu bar wins), so the button never fired and the reported hotkey did nothing. The button no longer claims a colliding mnemonic and announces the reliable transport key, Ctrl+P, which works regardless of focus. (apps/radio.py)

  • TuneIn stations can be added to Favorites from Browse Stations (#1210). TuneIn leaves resolve their stream lazily at play time, so the “Add to Favorites” action was missing for them (present for every other category). Add to Favorites — via the context menu or the favorite button — now resolves the TuneIn stream on demand off the UI thread, then adds the resolved station. (ui/radio/browse_tree_dialog.py)

  • Radio audio no longer keeps playing after the app exits (#1195, mpv engine).
