All user-facing docs (user guide, release notes, control reference, PRD, keybinding standard) were verified against quill/ui/main_frame_menu.py, quill/core/keymap.py, and quill/core/feature_catalog.py. Locked-off features (BITS Whisperer, GLOW) were removed from the user-facing docs and preserved in docs/planning/deferred-locked-features.md; offline-speech menu paths were corrected to the flat Tools > Speech (the gated “Whisperer” submenu no longer appears in docs); and stale keybindings/menu names were fixed to match the code — AI Grammar and Style (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+G), Translate Selection (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+T), AI Thesaurus (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+H), Compare navigation (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+. / , / D), Verbosity Quiet/Meeting/Undo (no default key), Replace (Ctrl+H), Browser Preview (QUILL Key + V), List submenu under Insert (not Format), the Advanced Tools submenu (was documented as “Power Tools”), and Keymap Editor (was “Keyboard Manager”).
  • No deferred-feature brand shows in a default build. The offline watch-folder action was renamed “Transcribe audio (Whisperer)” → “Transcribe audio (offline)” (quill/core/watch_transcribe.py); and the About-dialog overview paragraph, the Transcription settings-group description, and the trust-consent disclosure were de-branded so they no longer name the deferred BITS Whisperer suite or GLOW. The full BITS Whisperer suite (menu, dialogs, provider/rollout surfaces) remains in the code but gated off behind the locked core.bw_whisperer flag for QUILL 2.0, so none of it renders today.
  • Dead keybinding fixed. New Sticky Note (tools.sticky_note_capture) defaulted to the QUILL-key chord N, which the prefix handler intercepts for browse mode (so the chord never fired). It is now QUILL Key + Shift+N in DEFAULT_KEYMAP and the default/screen-reader keymap profiles.

One dialog for batch speech and audiobooks

  • Build Audiobook from Folder is folded into Batch Export to Speech Audio. The two near-identical dialogs are now one. In Tools > Speech > Batch Export to Speech Audio…, tick Assemble the results into one audiobook to reveal the book tags (title/author/narrator/genre/year), a cover-image picker, the book format (M4B/MP3), an ACX-loudness option, and a save-as path. After your documents are synthesized — one chapter each — the produced (and any pre-recorded) audio in the folder is combined into a single chaptered book. The standalone Build Audiobook dialog and its menu item were removed; the build logic (quill/core/speech/audiobook.py) is unchanged.
  • The “Kokoro speed” control now announces its name. The speed spinner is a SpinCtrlDouble, whose embedded edit field did not inherit its adjacent label, so screen readers read it unnamed on Tab; it (and the Rate spinner) now carry explicit accessible names.
  • Choose where temporary files live, and keep a log. A new Temporary files folder field puts each run’s scratch dir under a folder you pick (blank = system temp). A timestamped diagnostic log is created in the output folder before conversion starts and records discovery, per-document progress and timings, skips, errors, and the audiobook build. An optional Save the text sent to speech checkbox writes a <doc>.spoken.txt sidecar of the exact text each document speaks.
  • A real progress dialog with a percentage. Conversion now shows a focused, screen-reader-announced progress dialog whose percentage is words processed divided by total words, and which can be minimized to the status bar like other long operations. It opens after the configuration dialog closes so screen-reader focus reliably lands on it, and it advances per audio chunk — so even a single long document (or one with no headings) shows real movement instead of sitting on “Preparing…”.
  • Fixed a heading-less document appearing to hang. A Word document with no headings is one large section; it was handed to Kokoro in one oversized call (far past Kokoro’s small context window), which stalled. Neural engines now synthesize in much smaller chunks (Kokoro especially), so these documents complete and report steady progress.
  • Review chapters before building. Tick Review chapters (rename/reorder/merge) before building to open a chapter editor after synthesis; assembling a folder of pre-recorded audio always opens it. Rename, reorder, and merge chapters, then build. (This is the editor from the old standalone Build Audiobook dialog, brought back into the consolidated flow.)
  • Accessibility: spinner and list labels. The “Kokoro speed” control is a composite whose inner edit field is what a screen reader focuses; its accessible name now reaches that inner field. The round-robin voice list and translated-languages list got adjacent labels so they are announced by name on Tab.

Community fixes

Contributed by Kelly Ford:

  • New document tabs land focused, not blank. Opening a document via Open from URL, Import, or Open from Remote could leave the new tab’s editor unfocused and visually blank until you manually tabbed out and back — the closing modal dialog (a file, URL-entry, or remote-sites dialog) was restoring focus to the previous tab. The new tab now reliably receives focus, matching the normal Open flow (#805).
  • The Pandoc download shows a real, cancelable progress dialog. The first time a conversion needs Pandoc, its on-demand download now opens the same accessible percentage dialog as the other on-demand downloads (ffmpeg, offline speech engines) — with a Cancel button and screen-reader-announced progress — instead of a status-bar line that was easy to miss (#807).

Post to Mastodon, straight from the editor

  • Publish to Mastodon without leaving QUILL. Press QUILL Key + Shift+P (or Tools > Share > Post to Mastodon…): QUILL takes your selection — or the whole document if nothing is selected — and opens a compose dialog where you edit the text, pick which account to post from, choose visibility (Public / Unlisted / Followers-only / Direct), and see a live character count. Posts appear “via QUILL”. This is a lean poster, not a full client (no timelines or media in this first version).
  • Multiple accounts, with friendly names. Tools > Share > Mastodon Accounts… lets you add accounts (each with a nickname shown in the picker), set a default, and remove one. Adding an account registers QUILL on your server and uses a one-time browser authorization (you paste a short code back). Sign-in secrets are stored in the Windows Credential Manager / DPAPI, never in a plain file. New wx-free quill/core/mastodon/ package (single audited urllib egress site in client.py); dialogs in quill/ui/mastodon_dialogs.py.

Voice previews you can actually hear

  • Preview any voice with the Preview button — even before downloading it. In Manage Voices, selecting a voice and pressing Preview now: synthesises the preview phrase with the voice’s real model when it is downloaded, or plays a bundled pre-recorded sample (quill/data/voice-previews/) when it is not — so a Kokoro voice (or any not-yet-downloaded voice) can be auditioned before you commit to the ~114 MB download. The rate/volume/pitch/speed controls now dim until the voice is downloaded (they only affect real synthesis), and the wording points at the Preview button instead of an unreliable double-click/Enter. The eSpeak “Voice character” variant stays hidden for engines that don’t use it.

Suggest a filename from the first line

  • A new option pre-fills the Save dialog with a name taken from your document’s first line. Turn on Suggest a filename from the first line (Settings, General) and when you save an untitled document, QUILL proposes a name derived from the first line — across formats, stripping leading markup (a Markdown # heading, a > quote, a - list bullet, or inline HTML tags) and sanitizing it into a valid filename. Off by default; applies to Save As, Export, plain-text save, and DAISY. New wx-free quill/core/titles.py.

Sound packs you can actually pick

  • The first-run Sound-pack control is now a dropdown of the packs that ship with QUILL, replacing a *.qsp file picker that could never select them — the bundled packs are folders, not files, so the old picker was effectively broken. The dropdown defaults to QUILL’s own pack (Ink), so turning sound on always gives you working earcons. Bundled choices are stored as a stable bundled:<id> reference (or "" for the default) and resolved against the install, so a chosen pack survives a move or reinstall instead of going silent. New available_sound_packs() / resolve_sound_pack_path() in quill/core/sound_pack.py.

Upgrade hardening (durability for beta upgraders)

  • A corrupt config no longer crashes startup or silently vanishes. If settings.json or keymap.json is present but unparseable, QUILL now quarantines the original to migration-backups/<name>-corrupt-* and falls back to defaults, instead of raising on load (migration_backup.backup_corrupt_file).
  • Changed settings defaults can be pushed to existing users, like shortcuts. The recommended force-once mechanism now covers settings too (RecommendedSettingsUpdate), so an important changed default isn’t permanently pinned in an upgraded full-snapshot file. Same opt-out (Apply recommended keyboard-shortcut updates).
  • The persistence contract is now enforced by a gate. quill/tools/persistence_audit.py classifies every JSON-write site; a test fails if a new persisted store appears without deciding whether it needs the versioned-delta migration.
  • Every config store now carries a schema version. The backlog the gate surfaced is cleared: each previously-unversioned config file (remote/SSH sites, AI engine/model/provider config, publishing connections, quillin settings, read-only guards, profile-startup, deepgram, and more) now stores a schema_version (bare lists/maps wrap in a versioned envelope, with old files still loading), so a future shape change is migratable. And read_json is now corruption-tolerant globally — a malformed config file degrades to its default instead of raising, so one bad file can never crash a load path.

Upgrade hygiene: default hotkeys now reach existing users, and prior-install data can be imported

  • Changed and new default hotkeys now reach existing users automatically. keymap.json previously stored a full snapshot of every binding, so once you had used QUILL, a later change to a default shortcut never reached you — your file pinned the old value for every command. It is now stored as a delta of only your customizations plus a _defaults_epoch stamp, so any command you have not personally rebound always tracks the current default. This removes the fragile “add an old→new migration entry for every default change” tax: changing DEFAULT_KEYMAP is now enough. Legacy full-snapshot files are converted to the delta format once, on first load, and stamped — preserving your genuine customizations. (quill/core/keymap.py.)
  • Find is forced back to Ctrl+F on upgrade. As part of that legacy conversion, several pre-release builds that defaulted Find (edit.find) to a QUILL-key leader chord (e.g. QUILL Key + Z) are corrected: any leader-chord Find binding in a legacy keymap is reset to Ctrl+F, in place, with no data migration required. A non-leader Find you set deliberately (e.g. Ctrl+Alt+F), and any leader-chord Find you choose after upgrading, are left alone.
  • Settings now carry forward across upgrades, with a recoverable backup. settings.json was a full snapshot, so a changed default never reached you and a new setting only arrived through code. It is now a delta of just your changes plus a schema_version stamp, so new settings arrive at their defaults automatically, changed defaults reach you unless you overrode them, and your customizations are preserved. The first launch after upgrade converts an old file once; the original is copied to migration-backups/ first so the conversion is always reversible. Shared, reusable plumbing lives in the new wx-free quill/core/versioned_store.py and quill/core/migration_backup.py; settings serialization is in quill/core/settings_migration.py (schema_version 2). The contract is documented in docs/design/persistence-and-migration.md.
  • Recommended updates: important default changes can be pushed once, and you can opt out. When a default change matters enough to apply even to people who already customized it — restoring Find to Ctrl+F is the first — a recommended update sets it once, then never touches it again, so you remain free to rebind. Turn the whole mechanism off with Apply recommended keyboard-shortcut updates in Settings if you would rather keep your own choices untouched. (quill/core/recommended_updates.py.)
  • You choose how upgrades are surfaced, and can undo a shortcut change. A new rich Upgrade notice setting (migration_notice, Administration group: Silent / Brief announcement (default) / Summary with Undo) controls what QUILL says when it migrates your settings or applies a recommended shortcut fix on update. The Summary with Undo dialog and the Undo Recent Shortcut Change command (tools.undo_recommended_updates) revert a correction like Find→Ctrl+F while leaving it from re-applying. The migrate-and-surface plumbing is shared (migration_backup.pop_recent_migrations, MainFrame._surface_migration_notice).
  • The installer no longer leaves a half-upgraded app. Inno Setup only overlays new files; modules renamed or removed between releases could linger next to new ones and crash on launch with ImportError/AttributeError. The installer now cleanly replaces the first-party quill package (and stray __pycache__) on every install, while leaving the embedded Python runtime and your %APPDATA%\Quill data untouched. (scripts/build_windows_distribution.py, [InstallDelete].)
  • Import data from a previous QUILL install. When QUILL starts in a fresh data location but finds a populated data directory from an earlier install elsewhere (typically after switching between the portable and installed builds, or when the storage-mode marker was lost on reinstall), the first-run flow now offers to import your settings, keyboard shortcuts, and documents and restart to apply them. Detection and the move live in the wx-free quill/core/data_location.py (detect_importable_legacy_dir, apply_pending_legacy_import); the import is applied very early in quill.__main__.main() — before settings are read — and the location-control file (storage-mode.json) is intentionally left behind so the active data directory does not bounce back to the emptied source. Declining is remembered so you are not asked again.

0.7.0 Beta 1 (in development)

First pre-release of the 0.7.0 line. Tagged off release/0.7.0-verbosity once the remaining waves (#271 verbosity rebuild, #238-#246 braille phase 3-4, #300-#359 polish, planning-folder retirement) merge. The GitHub Pages update feed remains pointed at 0.5.0 so testers checking for updates do not see a phantom bump until 0.7.0 ships as a stable release. See RELEASE.md for the tag-time flip step and the full pre-tag checklist.

Hold-to-Dictate and Locked Dictation (F9 / Ctrl+F9)

  • Two keyboard-only ways to dictate into the editor, on the existing offline Whisper engine. Hold F9 to record and release to insert at the cursor as one undoable edit (Hold-to-Dictate); press Ctrl+F9 to start a locked session and again to finish (Locked Dictation, announced “Locked dictation on”). While recording, Escape stops and keeps the speech, Shift+Escape cancels and discards, Ctrl+Shift+F9 pauses/resumes a locked session, and Alt+F9 speaks the current state. All six shortcuts are remappable (Keymap Editor) and conflict-checked; Escape/Shift+Escape are only consumed while a session is active.
  • A Dictation Settings panel. Tools > Speech > Hold & Locked Dictation > Dictation Settings… exposes the dictation knobs the controller reads: the Locked-Dictation time limit, the minimum hold to start (to ignore accidental F9 taps), stop-and-keep-speech on focus loss, intelligent insertion spacing, and a reset to show the one-time first-use hint again. Accessible, keyboard-first; saves to settings.
  • A Dictation History & Review window, with startup recovery. Tools > Speech > Hold & Locked Dictation > Dictation History & Review… lists every dictation recording whose transcript was never inserted — crash orphans and transcripts that couldn’t be safely placed — so you can insert it at the cursor, copy it, or discard it (Enter inserts the selected row). On startup, if any recordings are awaiting review, QUILL announces it and points you here, so speech is never silently lost.
  • Locked Dictation now sounds distinct, and a one-time hint explains the keys. Locked Dictation plays its own distinct earcons (dictation_locked_on / dictation_locked_off, new in the bundled Ink pack) instead of sharing the Hold-to-Dictate transcription cues, so a sustained locked session is audibly different from a press-and-hold one. The first time you start dictation, QUILL speaks a one-time onboarding hint (the F9 / Ctrl+F9 keys and Escape behavior) and remembers not to repeat it (dictation_onboarding_shown setting).
  • Built as a protected transaction. New wx-free quill/core/speech/dictation/ package: an explicit DictationController state machine (no bare is_recording boolean; the single-recorder invariant is enforced), DictationSession/InsertionContext records, conservative insertion spacing, and a DictationRecoveryRepository that saves audio to recovery/dictation/ before transcription begins. Audio is secured before Whisper runs, a missing key-up cannot leave the microphone open (a watchdog polls physical key state), Locked Dictation stops at five minutes and on focus loss, the stop tone plays only after capture ends, and a transcript that cannot be safely inserted is kept for review rather than lost. The wx wiring lives in quill/ui/main_frame_dictation_hotkeys.py; MicRecorder gained pause/resume. Tests: tests/unit/core/speech/dictation/ (42) plus capture and feedback-table tests. Remaining follow-ups are tracked in docs/planning/program-tracker.md (Speech & Dictation).

Structured List Studio (F2)

  • Build and edit lists by concept, not syntax. A new context-sensitive F2 command (also Insert > List > Structured List Studio…) opens an accessible dialog that turns a selection into a list or starts a new one, supports Bulleted / Numbered / Checklist / Definition lists, and generates valid Markdown or HTML with a live read-only source preview — so the user never types -, 1., [ ], <ul>, or <dl>. Definition lists emit semantic <dl>/<dt>/<dd> (HTML) or a Pandoc-compatible syntax (Markdown). Inserting is one undoable edit. F2 moved Insert Special Character to Shift+F2 (both remappable, with a legacy-binding migration). New wx-free quill/core/lists/ package (model, import interpretation with ambiguity detection, renderers, conversions with information-loss detection, announcements) and the quill/ui/list_studio_dialog.py shell. This release covers flat lists and the common one-term/one-definition case; nested editing, multi-term/definition, import/conversion previews, and the full Settings/preset surface are planned follow-ups. Tests: tests/unit/core/lists/ (42) plus dialog-wiring tests. See docs/planning/quill-structured-list-studio-prd.md.

Document Language: edit anything as any language (#181)

  • A pinned Document Language now drives the editing characteristics, not just token navigation (#181). Setting a language (HTML on a plain .txt, say) makes Bold/Italic insert the right markup, enables the heading/table/list/tag menu items, switches comment toggling to that language’s syntax, and steers heading/structure navigation, the outline, link insertion, and live preview — all via a new pin-aware _effective_markup_kind()/_current_markup_context() and a markup_kind on LanguageProfile. Comment toggling (format_ops.toggle_line/block_comment) takes the profile so it wins over the file name. Set it with Ctrl+Shift+L, Navigate > Set Document Language…, the new Format > Document Language radio submenu, or Enter on the status-bar Language segment (which shows (set) when pinned). “Auto-detect from file” clears the override; a Save As hint fires when the language’s extension doesn’t match the file. Tab-only (not remembered across reopen). Added C# and PHP profiles.
  • Optional automatic language detection on paste/typing (#181). New wx-free quill/core/language_detect.py scores ~18 languages from weighted structural signals with VS Code-style confidence discipline (absolute floor, relative margin, ambiguity penalties for YAML/TOML/SQL, session-history bias, and a should_switch() hysteresis gate) — no ML, no dependency. The language_detection_mode setting (default off; hint/prompt/auto, in Settings > Editing) drives a debounced detector (main_frame_language_detect.py) that runs only on unpinned untitled/.txt documents, never overrides a real extension or a user choice, and — unlike a silent or visual-only switch — keeps a screen-reader user informed. Verified it does not misfire on plain prose or pasted ASCII-braille (BRF). Tests: test_language_detect.py (20 cases), plus markup_kind and profile-aware comment tests.

Quick Nav: misspellings and search hits as nav types (#513)

  • Go to Anything (Quick Nav) now lists misspellings and active search hits as navigable types. Alongside the existing landmarks (headings, links, lists, tables, block quotes, bookmarks, code blocks) and their live per-type counts, the unified Quick Nav panel now includes the document’s misspellings and — when a search is active — the current query’s search hits as their own categories, so you can filter to and jump straight to a misspelled word or a match from one place. The two transient types are computed on demand for the panel (via list_misspellings and core/search.find_matches) rather than in the prewarmed browse cache, so the perf-critical navigation cache stays light. New nav kinds in wx-free quill/core/quick_nav.py; a new _quick_nav_panel_context() augments the index in main_frame_browse.py. Tests: test_quick_nav.py (transient types in the index, count panel, filtering) and test_main_frame_navigation.py (the panel context surfaces misspellings + hits). Closes #513 (and folds in the undefined NAV-10 stub #578).

AI Voice: cloud TTS with Google Gemini and OpenAI

  • The AI Voice read-aloud and audio export now support Google Gemini 2.5 TTS in addition to OpenAI. A provider-neutral layer (quill/core/ai/cloud_tts.py) selects the backend, lists each provider’s models and voices (OpenAI 11 voices, Gemini 30), estimates the USD cost of a synthesis before it runs, and dispatches read-aloud/export. New quill/core/ai/gemini_tts.py calls the Gemini TTS REST API (the key travels in the x-goog-api-key header, never the URL), wraps its 24 kHz PCM into WAV, and is recorded in the network-egress audit. Pick the provider, model, and voice under Settings > Read Aloud; OpenAI exports MP3, Gemini exports WAV; “Export Document as MP3” is now “Export Document as Audio”.
  • ElevenLabs joins as a premium cloud-TTS provider for audio export. Export your document to ElevenLabs’ natural, audiobook-grade narration (MP3) by choosing ElevenLabs (export only) under Settings > Read Aloud. All ElevenLabs calls go through a single host-owned gateway (quill/core/ai/elevenlabs_tts.py) — the only module that imports the official elevenlabs SDK — which retrieves your stored ElevenLabs API key (the same credential the ElevenLabs transcription provider uses, never the environment), talks only to api.elevenlabs.io, translates SDK errors to clean messages, chunks long text on safe boundaries, and is cancelable. It ships as an optional extra (pip install quill[elevenlabs]) and is inert until installed and a key is set; Safe Mode and the AI Voice per-action consent both apply, and the gateway is recorded in the network-egress audit. A conservative cost estimate is shown before export. Scope note: this release covers ElevenLabs audio export; live per-sentence Read Aloud through ElevenLabs (streaming) is planned for a later release (roadmap §4.2).
  • Long documents are split on safe boundaries so synthesized audio never ends on a weird spot. A shared, boundary-safe splitter (quill/core/ai/tts_chunk.py, learned from the git-going-with-github Kokoro audiobook generator) normalizes whitespace and groups whole sentences under each provider’s per-request character cap, only ever falling back to a word boundary for a single over-long sentence — never mid-word. Gemini’s concatenated chunks get a short inter-chunk silence gap and a trailing tail so sentence endings are not clipped. Tests: test_cloud_tts.py (boundary safety, cost estimation, Gemini REST shape + error mapping, WAV header/tail, provider dispatch).

Read Aloud engines: SAPI 5 system voice, DECtalk fix, full voice previews

  • Windows system speech now runs on SAPI 5 directly; the pyttsx3 dependency is removed. Read Aloud’s “System TTS” engine and the screen-reader-less self-voicing announcement fallback (prism_bridge) both talk to the SAPI 5 SpVoice COM object through comtypes instead of pyttsx3, in a new wx-free quill/platform/windows/sapi5.py (voice enumeration, WAV file synthesis via SpFileStream, and a worker-thread singleton for announcements). This drops pyttsx3 and its runAndWait()-reuse external-loop workaround. The engine id is now sapi5; settings stored under the old pyttsx3 id migrate automatically, and SAPI token IDs match the old pyttsx3 voice IDs so saved voice selections carry over. comtypes is now a direct (Windows-only) dependency.
  • DECtalk Read Aloud actually works: synthesis is driven through DECtalk.dll, not the GUI speak.exe. The bundled DECtalk package’s AMD64\speak.exe is the graphical “Sample Speak Window”, not a console synthesizer — invoking it with -file/-wav/-dict made it fast-fail at startup (0xC000041D, STATUS_FATAL_USER_CALLBACK_EXCEPTION). A new self-contained console worker (quill/core/speech/dectalk_say.py) loads DECtalk.dll with DO_NOT_USE_AUDIO_DEVICE and writes a validated WAV; synthesize_to_file_with_dectalk runs it out-of-process (payload on stdin as Windows-1252, -w output), and DECtalk discovery now requires DECtalk.dll and rejects speak.exe. Both Read Aloud and preview generation share this path.
  • File synthesis no longer flashes a console window, and eSpeak-NG can’t hang the run. The Piper and eSpeak-NG file-synthesis subprocesses now launch with CREATE_NO_WINDOW, so batch and export runs no longer pop a console window between files (a real distraction for screen-reader users), and the eSpeak-NG file synthesis carries a timeout that surfaces a clean “did not complete” error instead of stalling the task.
  • eSpeak-NG no longer crashes for portable/managed installs. synthesize_with_espeak now passes --path to the co-located espeak-ng-data directory when present, so the in-app-downloaded and bundled portable eSpeak-NG synthesize cleanly instead of segfaulting (0xC0000005) when they cannot locate their data directory.
  • Kokoro catalog corrected to a real, CPU-only voice. The non-existent am_zeus (which silently fell through to a torch/GPU download) is replaced by am_santa, the actual 28th English voice present in the bundled CPU ONNX pack — so the full 28-voice catalog works on the offline ONNX path with no GPU.
  • Bundled voice previews now cover every catalog voice and share one phrase. All preview clips (Piper 37, Kokoro 28, eSpeak 8, DECtalk 9, plus the Windows SAPI voices) speak the same phrase from a single phrase.txt at the repo root, generated by scripts/gen_voice_previews.py (which now also covers SAPI 5 voices and returns a non-zero exit on any per-voice failure).

Batch Document-to-Speech: a folder of documents to audio

  • Convert a whole folder of documents to speech audio in one pass (Tools > Speech > Batch Export to Speech Audio). A keyboard-first dialog collects a source folder, the file types to include (.docx/.md/.html/.txt), the engine/voice/pace, the output format, and the chapter options, then runs the conversion on a background task with per-file progress and cancel. The conversion core is wx-free and headless-testable: quill/core/speech/batch_export.py (scan → extract → normalize → pronounce → polish → synthesize) plus batch_discovery.py (folder scan + filters) and batch_manifest.py (run report). Each document is read with the same shared pipeline live Read Aloud uses, so the voice you audition is the voice you get. Richer .docx extraction reads tables row by row (cells joined as “cell, cell”), pulls in footnotes (as a trailing “Footnotes” chapter) and header/footer text, and walks the body in order so a table never scrambles the surrounding prose.
  • Heading-aware chapterization with MP3 and M4B chapter markers. quill/core/speech/chapters.py writes ID3v2.3 CHAP/CTOC frames (the format Apple Podcasts, Overcast, VLC, foobar2000 navigate) so a long document becomes a navigable, chaptered audio file; chapter_assemble.py stitches heading sections with a configurable inter-article pause, an optional transition earcon — a natural page-turn cue by default (quill/assets/audio/page_turn.wav), or your configured batch_speech_chapter_sound_id from the active sound pack; any chosen or default sound is conformed (resampled, channel-mixed, width-matched) to the speech format so it always splices cleanly, with the synthesized chime only as a last resort — with a clean and a with-tones variant sharing identical chapter timing, spoken headings, inter-sentence pauses, and an anti-clipping tail pad. The batch dialog now also offers M4B audiobook output with native MP4 chapter atoms (via ffmpeg FFMETADATA in quill/core/speech/ffmpeg.py) — the Apple/audiobook format — built from the same chapter timing as the MP3 markers. A very long section is auto-chunked on safe sentence/word boundaries (via tts_chunk) so a single synthesis call never runs past the engine timeout, and the run reports each document’s chapter count (and a total in the summary). A Chapter mode choice adds Separate file per article — instead of one chaptered file, each heading-bounded section is written as its own NNN - <heading> audio file into a per-document folder. A Dry run option writes a <doc>.preview.txt of the exact spoken text — after normalization, pronunciation correction, and polish — for each document without synthesizing, reporting the pronunciation-substitution count, so you can review what will be said before paying for the (slow) synthesis.
  • Translated audio export — local and cloud voices, batch and single-document. Export a document’s audio in additional languages: pick a language (any of QUILL’s ~37 translatable languages) and a voice, Add language, and QUILL translates and writes <doc> (<Language>).<ext> alongside the original. Two surfaces: Batch Export to Speech (a whole folder) and a new single-document action, Tools > Speech > Export to Translated Speech Audio, for just the current document. Translation uses any configured AI provider (the export rides QUILL’s AI gateway) or LibreTranslate (local), with bounded-backoff retries that halt a language on persistent failure rather than emit half-translated audio, and a cache so repeated strings translate once (quill/core/speech/translate_sections.py). Voices are offered local-first via the per-language voice model (quill/core/speech/voice_languages.py): eSpeak NG speaks nearly every language fully offline (dialects like Spanish es / es-419), installed SAPI 5 voices are matched by their language (LCID), and the premium multilingual cloud voices (OpenAI / Gemini / ElevenLabs) are offered as the quality tier — they synthesize translated audio through cloud_tts and are conformed to WAV via ffmpeg so chapter splicing still works. Pronunciation dictionaries are language-aware — a dictionary scoped to a language (PronunciationDictionary.language) only affects that language’s synthesis. The full chaptered treatment (page-turn cue, chapters, combine-headings, ACX loudness) applies to each translated file. (Remembering the language list per project is the one remaining 2.0 nicety.)
  • Cost is surfaced before any metered cloud run. Because a translated cloud run can meter twice — to translate (paid cloud AI) and to synthesize (cloud TTS) — QUILL shows a combined estimate and asks to proceed first (quill/core/speech/cost_estimate.py): TTS is priced precisely per provider/model, translation is free for LibreTranslate or a local AI model and estimated from a blended per-character rate for a paid cloud provider. Local-only runs are never interrupted.
  • Voice-failure blacklist (batch robustness). A voice that fails synthesis — a broken SAPI token, a missing model, a rejected cloud voice id — is recorded and skipped on later runs, so one bad voice in a round-robin rotation no longer keeps aborting a batch. Persisted atomically in the app data dir (quill/core/speech/voice_blacklist.py); the round-robin rotation drops blacklisted ids and any new failure this run is remembered for next time.
  • Loudness normalization for batch export (ChapterForge-style). A Normalize loudness to audiobook (ACX) level checkbox brings the audiobook-grade loudness the audiobook builder already had to batch document-to-speech: each output is run through a two-pass loudnorm (measure with print_format=json, then apply seeded with the measured values, linear=true) applied to the assembled WAV before encoding, so MP3/M4B chapter markers survive and the timing stays exact (loudnorm preserves duration). Falls back to a single pass if the measurement can’t be parsed, and is a no-op (keeps your audio) when ffmpeg is absent. Persisted per project. New loudness.normalize_wav_loudness. Tests in test_loudness.py.
  • Combine empty headings, and round-robin voices. Two new Batch Export to Speech controls. Combine empty headings into the next article folds a heading that has no body of its own into the next article that does and joins their titles (e.g. “Part One: Chapter 1”), so you don’t get empty “chapter” announcements — the rule the ACB audio pipeline uses (text_polish.combine_heading_only_sections). Round-robin voices lets each article/heading be read by the next voice in a list you build: pick a voice from the engine’s voices, Add it to an ordered list, and reorder with Move Up / Move Down / Remove — then section i is voiced by voice i mod N (assemble_chaptered_audio(..., synthesizers=…)). The rotation uses voices of the selected engine (so every section shares one audio format and splices cleanly); switching engines clears it. Both options are remembered per project (.quill/speech-project.json). Tests: test_section_combining.py, round-robin assembler + dialog tests.
  • Configure-once-per-project speech settings. A project (a folder of documents) remembers its whole speech profile in <project>/.quill/speech-project.json (schema quill/core/schemas/speech_project.json): synthesizer + voice/pace, output, discovery, chapters, text normalization, the active pronunciation dictionaries, audiobook metadata, and execution policy. to_batch_options drives the batch pipeline straight from a project file, including headlessly in tests. The batch dialog now applies the project profile on open (so the folder you last tuned pre-fills its dialog) and auto-remembers your choices on Start, with a clear precedence (this run > project profile > global defaults), so per-project speech setup is genuinely configure-once.
  • Maximum flexibility knobs for batch conversion. Output now covers WAV, MP3, M4A, M4B (audiobook), Opus, FLAC, and OGG (compressed formats via ffmpeg, falling back to WAV with a per-file note when ffmpeg is absent), with a selectable MP3 VBR quality and an optional uniform WAV sample-rate / channel conform. Discovery gained include/exclude glob filters and a max-file-size cap. The existing-file policy is now skip / overwrite / rename (rename keeps both as name (2).ext). Output layout supports flatten and a filename template ({index:03d} - {stem}). Compressed outputs are stamped with audiobook metadata (album, author/narrator, genre, year; per-file title from the heading/stem, track from the index). Error handling adds stop-on-error and per-file retry; throughput adds parallel workers (clamped to 1 for the single-apartment SAPI 5 / Kokoro engines); and an optional manifest.json/.csv records every file’s status, output, duration, and errors. Per-engine shaping is exposed for eSpeak (pitch, word gap) and Piper (length/noise scales). Tests: test_batch_export_flexibility.py, test_batch_export_formats.py, test_project_profile.py.

Build Audiobook from a folder (ChapterForge)

  • Combine a folder of audio files into one chaptered audiobook (Tools > Speech > Build Audiobook from Folder…). The ChapterForge-aligned surface (design source: the sibling ChapterForge project): point QUILL at a folder of audio files and it builds a single chaptered MP3 (ID3 CHAP markers) or M4B audiobook (native MP4 chapter atoms) master — each file becomes a chapter, titles are derived from the filenames in natural-sort order, a cover image in the folder is auto-detected, and the book’s tags (title/author/narrator/genre/year) are written. New wx-free quill/core/speech/audiobook.py (folder scan, natural sort, title-from-filename, cover detection, ffprobe duration, and the ffmpeg concat-demuxer build) behind the keyboard-first quill/ui/audiobook_builder_dialog.py, run on the background task pool. Tests: test_audiobook.py (scan/sort/titles/cover + the concat command builders). Only the surfaces that fit QUILL’s audiobook vision are ported; ChapterForge’s Auphonic, RSS-feed, SFTP-publish, and metadata-lookup features are intentionally left out.
  • Edit the chapters before building — rename, reorder, merge. The builder now lists the chapters and lets you rename the selected one (inline title field, Enter or the Rename button), move it up or down, or merge it into the chapter above (two source files then play as one chapter with a single marker — AudiobookChapter.extra_paths). The edited plan rides through AudiobookRequest.chapter_plan into audiobook.chapters_from_plan; the chapter list is keyboard-activatable (GATE-13).
  • One-click ACX loudness compliance. A Normalize loudness to ACX (Audible/audiobook) spec checkbox runs an ffmpeg loudnorm pass during the build (so chapters and tags are preserved), targeting the ACX window (RMS between -23 and -18 dB, peak under -3 dB). After the build QUILL measures the finished master with volumedetect and reports the RMS/peak verdict in the completion status. New wx-free quill/core/speech/loudness.py; tests in test_loudness.py. (FLAC/Opus audiobook output is intentionally not offered — neither format carries chapter markers; both remain accepted as source files. Direct publishing to WordPress/etc. is tracked for a later release.)

Pronunciation dictionaries: teach QUILL how to say things

  • Per-engine, global-or-project pronunciation dictionaries applied everywhere speech happens. A new wx-free core (quill/core/speech/pronunciation.py, schema pronunciation.json) stores ordered substitution rules (literal or regex, case-sensitive optional, per-engine scoping) in global (app-data) and project (<project>/.quill/pronunciation/) dictionaries. Corrections are applied as a silent text transform before synthesis in both batch export and live Read Aloud — the same stage in one shared pipeline — so a fix made once is heard everywhere. A Manage Pronunciations… dialog (Tools > Speech) edits entries with live Read-Aloud preview and audio audition, and a bundled starter dictionary ships common tech/brand pronunciations out of the box.

TTS text normalization: clean text in, clean speech out

  • Configurable, deterministic text cleanup before synthesis (quill/core/speech/text_normalize.py). Real documents are full of typography that makes engines stumble — curly quotes, em/en dashes, ellipses, invisible spaces, soft hyphens, ligatures, bullets, symbols, fractions, emoji, control characters — and of structured tokens that must be spoken clearly. Normalization runs first in the shared pipeline with excellent defaults and rich per-pass options, including per-type speaking profiles for phone numbers, emails, and URLs (announce / speak / speak-then-repeat, with a long-URL threshold). It also speaks publication shorthand (Vol.→“Volume”, No.→“Number”) and resolution-style numbers (2025-02→“2025 dash 2”), both on by default and opt-out (the ACB pipeline learnings). It is opt-in via the tts_normalization setting and carried per project in the speech profile.

SSML Builder and native SSML playback

  • Author guided pronunciation/prosody without hand-writing SSML. A new SSML Builder dialog composes <speak> markup (emphasis, breaks, say-as, phoneme, prosody) from accessible controls with a read-only source preview, for fine-grained control over how a passage is spoken.
  • Native SSML playback on SAPI 5 and eSpeak (Phase 6). Read Aloud now renders assembled <speak> utterances natively on SAPI 5 and eSpeak-NG (eSpeak via its markup mode) instead of reading the tags aloud, so <break>, emphasis, and prosody take effect; SSML utterances are passed through intact, bypassing punctuation verbalization that would corrupt the markup.

Offline speech: model manager, formats, and reliability

  • Model downloads no longer freeze the app, and show real progress (#617). FasterWhisperProvider.is_available() did a full import faster_whisper (which pulls in CTranslate2 and can take ~25 s on first load) on the UI thread whenever the Speech menu built its registry — freezing the whole app. It now probes with importlib.util.find_spec (~2 ms); the heavy import stays in the transcription path, which runs on a worker thread. Model downloads run on a worker thread behind a cancelable progress dialog with a real percentage and screen-reader milestone announcements (Faster Whisper downloads report byte-level progress via a tqdm subclass). Cancel is cooperative — the progress callback raises, aborting the stream and cleaning up the partial file.
  • Discoverable model deletion + machine-fit guidance (#617). Manage Speech Models now shows an explicit Download / Remove action picker (deletion is no longer inferred silently from install state). The header shows a one-line machine summary (RAM + GPU); each model estimates the RAM it needs and is flagged when it exceeds available memory; the best fit is marked “Recommended for your computer”; heavy models note when no GPU is present; and the download prompt warns on low disk space before starting. Fit/recommendation/disk logic is wx-free in quill/core/speech/service.py (unit-tested), reusing the offline-speech RAM/GPU/disk probes.
  • Transcribe almost any audio or video format (#617). whisper.cpp only reads 16 kHz mono WAV, so other inputs (MP3, M4A, MP4, MOV, MKV, …) could silently fail. When ffmpeg is available QUILL now transcodes the file to a temporary 16 kHz mono WAV first via the new wx-free quill/core/speech/ffmpeg.py (bundled-first-then-PATH resolver, basename allowlist, runs through run_subprocess_safely so no console window flashes); Faster Whisper decodes these formats itself. Without ffmpeg, QUILL passes a .wav through and gives a clear, actionable message for other formats. The Transcribe/Captions pickers were widened to the full format list. ffmpeg is GPL/LGPL and QUILL does not bundle it (non-redistributor stance) — it uses a system or user-installed copy.
  • Offline speech lives under Tools > Speech (#617). The on-device transcription, captions, dictation, and voice-command items moved out of the AI menu — they need no AI account or key, so they no longer imply an AI dependency. The Windows installer’s optional speechwhisper component (whisper.cpp engine payload under tools\speech\whispercpp) is now emitted by the installer-script generator so a build keeps the offline engine.
  • Hugging Face download hardening + transparency (#617). Three changes to how speech models are fetched: (1) the model manager shows each model’s license and a model-card URL in the row label and the download confirm (service.model_card_url derives it from either the whisper.cpp resolve URL or the FW repo id); (2) supply-chain pinning — whisper.cpp models download from a pinned commit with a per-file sha256 (the existing verifier now runs), Faster Whisper repos pin a revision (new SpeechModelInfo.revision), and the stale tinydiarize speaker-detection URL (which 404’d against ggerganov/whisper.cpp) was fixed to akashmjn/tinydiarize-whisper.cpp; (3) an optional Hugging Face token (new wx-free quill/core/speech/hf_auth.py, reusing the QUILL:huggingface:token credential) is attached to both download paths when present, a 429 becomes a clear “add a token or retry” message, and Tools > Speech > Hugging Face Token... sets it via a masked dialog. A token is not required for the public MIT models. Tests: test_hf_auth.py, plus catalog-pinning and license/card-url assertions.
  • Watch Folder can now auto-transcribe arriving audio on-device (WATCH-9). A new Transcribe audio (offline) watch action runs whisper.cpp / Faster Whisper entirely on your machine over each audio or video file a watch profile claims, writing a sibling .txt transcript — nothing is uploaded, so it needs no consent (the on-device counterpart to the existing cloud Transcribe audio (OpenAI Whisper) action). It replaces the old “not available yet” placeholder, is discoverable in the watch action picker, and reports clear guidance (open Tools > Speech > Manage Speech Models) when no offline model is installed rather than failing silently. New wx-free quill/core/speech/transcribe.py picks the best available offline provider and model; the two transcription watch actions moved into a new quill/core/watch_transcribe.py to keep watch_actions.py within its size budget. Tests: test_transcribe.py (8) and the WhispererTranscribeAction cases in test_watch_actions.py. This is the first item delivered from the offline-speech consolidation workstream.
  • Choose the transcript output format: Text, SubRip (.srt), WebVTT (.vtt), or Markdown (WATCH-9). The offline transcribe watch action now honors an output_format option and writes the sibling file with the matching extension, reusing speech/formatters.py (the action returns the full timestamped result, not just text). SRT/VTT carry timestamps and fall back to plain text when the engine returns no segments, so a profile never writes an empty caption file. A Transcript format picker was added to the Add/Edit Watch Profile dialog. To stay under the GATE-11 size budget, that dialog’s _edit_watch_profile was extracted from main_frame.py into a new quill/ui/main_frame_watch_profile.py (WatchProfileDialogMixin), ratcheting the main_frame.py budget down by ~430 lines.
  • Cloud transcription providers can now be added by Quillins (extensions). A new declarative transcription_providers manifest contribution lets a Quillin add a cloud speech-to-text provider (e.g. OpenAI Whisper) to QUILL’s speech engine, surfaced wherever you transcribe a file. It is host-mediated and least-privilege: the Quillin ships no code and needs no net capability — it only declares the provider, and the QUILL host performs the upload through its network-egress-audited transcription path, so the sandbox never handles audio bytes or the API key. Only host-vetted kinds are accepted, so a manifest can never point the host at an arbitrary endpoint. Cloud providers are network-backed and excluded from the offline paths — the offline Watch Folder transcribe action only ever uses on-device engines, so audio is never silently uploaded — and they are unavailable in Safe Mode or without a configured key. New: quill/core/speech/quillin_providers.py (host adapter + registry), the transcription_providers model/validation/schema, and the bundled reference Quillin openai-whisper-transcription. This is the next step in the offline-speech consolidation (#669) — shipping the cloud provider matrix as Quillins rather than core. See docs/quillins/quillins.md.
  • New optional offline engine: Vosk (English, very low resource) (#669). A third on-device engine for old or constrained machines: Vosk (Kaldi) is CPU-only and runs on a ~40 MB English model with no GPU — the accessibility-reach option where the heavier engines are impractical. Because its wheel is self-contained (it ships libvosk), it now bundles in the Windows installer (added to the build’s base dependency groups), so installer users get it out of the box — no separate component or native staging, unlike whisper.cpp; from source it is the vosk extra (pip install -e ".[vosk]"). Like the others it is never imported at startup. Two Apache-2.0 models (vosk-model-small-en-us-0.15 ~40 MB, vosk-model-en-us-0.22 ~1.8 GB) download from alphacephei.com via Manage Speech Models over verified HTTPS, MD5-pinned and zip-slip-guarded on extract; the new outbound host is recorded in the network-egress audit. Word-level timestamps are aggregated into segments. Fully offline, so the offline Watch Folder transcribe action can use it. New: quill/core/speech/providers/vosk.py + catalog entries; tests in test_vosk_provider.py.
  • Two more cloud transcription providers, on a config-driven adapter: Groq Whisper and ElevenLabs Scribe (#669). Building the cloud provider matrix as Quillins continues. Most cloud speech APIs share a shape — POST audio to an HTTPS endpoint with an API-key header, read the transcript from a known JSON path — so a new wx-free quill/core/speech/cloud_transcribers.py describes each as a data-only RestSpec and performs the call generically (transcribe_rest), making a new kind a data entry rather than new code. Two are added now: Groq (whisper-large-v3-turbo, the same Whisper model run on Groq’s hardware for very fast turnaround) and ElevenLabs Scribe (scribe_v1, with optional speaker diarization). A single generic RestCloudProvider host adapter serves every REST kind, selecting its endpoint by kind; the one urlopen call site is the single reviewed network-egress entry for all of them (HTTPS enforced, verified TLS). The transcription_providers manifest enum and validator allowlist grew to openai_whisper/groq/elevenlabs, and two bundled declarative Quillins ship (groq-transcription, elevenlabs-transcription) — each purely declarative, no net capability. Like all cloud providers they are network-backed, excluded from the offline paths, and unavailable in Safe Mode or without a key. Tests: test_cloud_transcribers.py (REST call shape, header schemes, raw vs multipart body, HTTPS enforcement, 401 mapping, JSON-path extraction, kind/allowlist consistency) and the RestCloudProvider routing case in test_quillin_providers.py.
  • Faster Whisper can now be installed on demand, no source checkout needed (#669). The higher-throughput, GPU-capable Faster Whisper engine pulls in ~110 MB of binary dependencies (CTranslate2, ONNX Runtime, PyAV, tokenizers), so it is not in the installer payload. Tools > Speech > Download Faster Whisper Engine… now installs it on an explicit action: wheel-only (pip install --only-binary=:all:) into a user-writable engine-pack folder (<app data>/engine-packs/faster-whisper, no admin), which is prepended to sys.path at startup so the engine then appears in the Speech Engine chooser. Blocked in Safe Mode, behind a confirmation + progress dialog, and the pip-to-PyPI network touch is documented in the network-egress audit. The shipped runtime now keeps pip (setuptools/wheel are still pruned; a wheel-only install needs no build backend) to power this. New wx-free quill/core/speech/engine_install.py; tests in test_engine_install.py (command shape, Safe Mode block, unsupported-runtime guard, pip-failure surfacing, post-install import verification, sys.path activation).

Text-file round-trip and accessibility fixes

  • UTF-8 BOM no longer leaks into the buffer (#648). quill.io.text.read_text_document reads raw bytes, detects a UTF-8 BOM, decodes with utf-8-sig (so no editable U+FEFF), and stores utf-8-sig as the encoding so the BOM is re-added byte-for-byte on save. Byte-exact round-trip tests in tests/unit/io/test_text.py.
  • CRLF and blank-line runs preserved on save (#649). Line endings are now detected from the raw bytes (the previous path.read_text() translated CRLF→LF before detection, so it always mis-detected LF). quill.io.export.write_document_as writes .txt/.text verbatim instead of routing them through markdown_to_plain_text, which collapsed runs of 3+ newlines to 2; the explicit Save As Plain Text command still flattens markup on purpose.
  • AI Hub tab group has an accessible name (#643/#646). The AIHubDialog notebook now sets an explicit accessible name so VoiceOver announces the settings dialog’s tab group (best-effort; macOS verification pending). GATE-11 budget rebaselined accordingly.

Editing and keyboard polish

  • List boxes are now keyboard-activatable, not just double-click (GATE-13). A wx.ListBox emits no item-activated event, so dialogs that bound only EVT_LISTBOX_DCLICK left their action unreachable for keyboard and screen-reader users. A new shared apply_listbox_activation helper (quill/ui/dialog_contract.py) binds the double-click and Enter / NumpadEnter / Space (consuming the key so the dialog’s default button does not also fire), applied across the SSH, Copy Tray (×2), Prompt Library, Remote Sites (×2), Skill Library, Publishing, and Pronunciation manager dialogs. A new gate (check_listbox_activation.py, GATE-13) fails CI if a future EVT_LISTBOX_DCLICK binding ships without keyboard activation.
  • Tab key can now insert a literal tab character (QUILL Key + U). The Tab key still defaults to Quill’s smart line indent, but a new session toggle switches it to insert a tab character at the caret like a plain text editor. Toggle it with the QUILL Key + U chord (Ctrl+M and Ctrl+Shift+M are taken by the mark ring, and Ctrl+Alt+ chords are screen-reader-hostile), via the checkable Format → Tab Key Inserts Tab Character menu item, or by activating the new Tab Mode status-bar cell (shows Indent / Tab char). The mode change is spoken. Shift+Tab continues to outdent even in tab-character mode. New format.toggle_tab_insert_mode command bound in both keymap profiles.
  • Tab indent is spoken under a screen reader again. A Tab/Shift+Tab indent (and Markdown list nest/promote) mutates text without moving focus, so the announcement engine — which stays silent while JAWS/NVDA is active to avoid talking over them — dropped the “Indented lines” status. The indent path now forces the announcement (the same escape hatch the QUILL-key chord uses) so the action is confirmed aloud.
  • F6 reaches the document tab bar. When Show Tab Control is on, the tab strip now joins the F6 region rotation as the Document Tabs region (Editor → Document Tabs → Preview → Status Bar) instead of being unreachable from the keyboard and the JAWS cursor.
  • Clear All Notifications from the status bar. The notifications (and background-tasks) status-cell context menu gained a Clear All Notifications entry that empties the list without opening the dialog.
  • Spoken confirmations for results that don’t move focus. A new internal escape hatch (_announce_result) force-speaks explicit command results that change nothing for the screen reader to pick up. F7 / F8 (next/previous misspelling) now speak “No misspellings ahead; N behind” (and the reverse) instead of going silent when the caret is already past the last misspelling, and Check for External Changes speaks “matches the on-disk version” when there is no change.
  • Ctrl+W closes the active document when no side preview is open. Previously Ctrl+W only closed a split side-preview and otherwise did nothing; it now falls back to closing the current document (only while focus is in the document surface, so dialogs are unaffected).
  • Search menu no longer announces a stray “Separator”. A feature-gated section left a dangling menu separator the screen reader read but could not focus; dangling leading/trailing/doubled separators are now pruned.
  • Watch Queue Monitor explains itself when unavailable. Opening it in a profile without the watch-folder feature now speaks “Watch folder is unavailable in this profile” instead of returning to the editor with only a silent status.
  • Escape cancels the speech-engine chooser cleanly. Pressing Escape on the Manage Speech Models engine picker now returns to the editor instead of falling through to the default engine’s model list.
  • Optionally drop missing recent files (recent_files_auto_clear_missing). A new General setting (off by default) removes Recent Files entries whose file no longer exists — but only on confirmed fixed/internal drives. Entries on removable, USB, or network drives are never probed and never dropped, because a “missing” file there usually means the drive is disconnected, not deleted.
  • Abbreviation expansion now ships off by default. So text never silently changes as you type without your knowledge, abbreviation_expansion now defaults to off. Turn it on whenever you want it — Insert > Toggle Abbreviation Expansion (Ctrl+Shift+Grave, E), the ABR status-bar cell, or Abbreviation expansion in Preferences > Editing — and the choice is remembered. The fifteen built-in abbreviations and the manager are unchanged; they simply don’t fire until you opt in.

Removed

  • Pandoc Conversion Center menu placeholder removed. The Tools menu no longer carries the “coming soon” Pandoc Conversion Center item; File → Import and File → Export (plus Tools → Batch Conversion for folders) cover conversion.

Verbosity system (live wiring + polish)

  • Announcement anti-spam: repetition collapse + budget (#408/#409). QUILL no longer re-speaks the exact same announcement when it repeats within a moment — holding a key at the top or bottom of a list, a repeated “no more results”, a chattering status line — and an optional announcement budget caps how many announcements are spoken in a rolling 5-second window (0 = no cap). Both run at the verbosity controller’s single process() choke-point (new wx-free quill/core/verbosity/throttle.py, an injectable clock for deterministic tests) and affect only the spoken channel — the visual status bar always shows every update, so nothing is hidden. Two new searchable settings under Preferences > Accessibility: Collapse repeated announcements (on by default) and Announcement budget (per 5 seconds). Tests: test_verbosity_throttle.py + controller integration. GATE-11 rebaselined (settings.py +11, settings_specs.py +20). This completes the high-value verbosity polish for 1.0; the speculative long-tail addenda are deferred to 2.0 (roadmap §5).
  • Verbosity rebuild — wired into the editor and reachable (#365), and the 100-item polish consolidated (#366). Sub-PR 1.5 makes the system live: a VerbosityController (wx-free, in quill/core/verbosity/controller.py) owns the engine, Quiet/Meeting modes, the undo stack, announcement history, mastery, and Safe Mode; MainFrame gains a VerbosityCommandsMixin and registers eight verbosity.* commands (palette- and Keyboard-Manager-reachable). _announce routes through the controller only once the user engages verbosity, so the default path is unchanged until then; once live, Quiet/Meeting suppress speech while the status-bar floor remains. Default chords (no conflicts; quote_lines keeps Ctrl+Shift+Q): Quiet = QUILL key + Q, Meeting = QUILL key + Shift+Q, Verbosity Undo = Ctrl+Shift+Z. Eight scalar verbosity_* settings persist (mastery, validation mode, history, task-profile suggestions, Safe Mode); collection-typed state lives in verbosity_custom.json. Tests: test_verbosity_controller.py, test_verbosity_settings.py, test_verbosity_wiring.py (incl. a quote_lines regression guard). GATE-11 rebaselined (settings.py 933→971, main_frame.py 25295→25355). Sub-PR 1.6 (the “100-item addendum”) was consolidated during the 2026-06-21 tracker cleanup into the deduplicated Polish backlog in docs/planning/verbosity-system.md rather than built as 100 separate items: the themed survivors are tracked there (several already shipped as settings knobs and profile recipes), and the screen-reader-redundant ideas (typing/command echo, speech-rate knobs, punctuation profiles) are recorded as “recommend do not build.” Live in-app behavior (chords firing, mode suppression, badges) warrants a manual smoke run before release.

Verbosity system (the UI)

  • Verbosity rebuild — the wxPython UI landed (#364). Sub-PR 1.4 adds the user-facing surfaces, all A11Y-4 hardened (label-then-control, mnemonics, SetName/SetHint, apply_modal_ids, no icon-only buttons) and wired to the already-tested pure core. New under quill/ui/: verbosity_prefs.py (the embeddable preferences panel — profile picker, the four-channel mix with the always-on Visual floor, validation-mode and mastery boxes, a filterable verb table with master/detail, initial focus on the filter), verbosity_token_editor.py (Simple/Advanced RadioBox view per the §5 decision, Ctrl+T validate / Ctrl+Shift+P preview via EVT_CHAR_HOOK, Save disabled while errors exist, Speak-current-template and Insert-token), verbosity_data_order.py (Move Up/Down/Reset/Preview), verbosity_chord_editor.py (per-chord overrides), verbosity_library.py (template CRUD + Install QVP), verbosity_qvp_install.py (pick → validate → install with the spoken sequence and accepted/rejected/warnings read-back), verbosity_history.py (review/replay/copy/explain with a filter), verbosity_preview_lab.py (the 14 scenarios with per-channel output), verbosity_safe_mode.py (scoped non-destructive resets), and verbosity_import_export.py (data-only profile import/export). The 3-tab About dialog already ships (About Quill, #260). The dialog inventory was regenerated (12 new hardened surfaces) and the A11Y-4 banned-pattern, button-contract, escape-z-order, and GATE-11 gates all pass. Tests: tests/unit/ui/test_verbosity_ui.py (62 source-contract cases). The dialogs are not yet launched from menus/chords — that wiring (and the user-guide/release-notes coverage) is sub-PR 1.5 (#365).

Verbosity system (QVP packs, library, preview)

  • Verbosity rebuild — QVP packs, template library, and scenario preview landed (#363, closes §20 #382). Sub-PR 1.3 adds the shareable-pack and preview core, still wx-free. New quill/core/schemas/qvp.json defines the canonical .qvp.json format (§20). quill/core/verbosity/qvp.py loads and validates packs by hand (QUILL ships no jsonschema runtime dependency) and runs the §21 install flow — JSON → schema → kind → min_quill_version gate → metadata → template ids → namespace collisions → dependency check → validate each template against its target verb → install → announce — returning a QVPInstallResult (accepted, rejected_templates with reasons, warnings, and the spoken sequence). Packs are strictly data: there is no exec/eval/__import__ path, asserted by test. library.py is the templates library: a flat collection across built-in / user / QVP sources with save/rename/delete CRUD and the cross-verb apply that strips tokens a verb doesn’t track and reports them (“Removed 2 tokens because this verb does not track them: cell, region.”). preview.py renders fourteen canned scenarios through the engine, surfacing per-channel output and decision metadata for the Preview Lab. schema.py’s QVP schema now loads from the canonical qvp.json (single source of truth). Tests: test_verbosity_qvp.py, test_verbosity_library.py, test_verbosity_preview.py, plus golden snapshots in tests/golden/verbosity/ (220 verbosity cases total; qvp coverage 95%). Partial progress on §19/§21/§23 (#381/#383/#385) whose CRUD/install-dialog/lab UIs land with the UI sub-PR.

Verbosity system (engine and runtime modes)

  • Verbosity rebuild — engine, modes, history, and stores landed (#362, closes §30 #392). Sub-PR 1.2 builds on the foundation with the central routing engine and its companions, still wx-free and strict-typed in quill/core/verbosity/. No user-facing surface yet (chords, badges, and dialogs come with the UI sub-PR), but the decision layer is complete and the engine is now reachable from the assistant-panel and AI-Hub announce paths via a no-op speak_legacy_text() passthrough. New modules: engine.py (VerbosityEngine.speak() → RenderedAnnouncement + ExplanationTrace; decides template precedence per-chord → per-verb → QVP → default, routes channels with the always-on visual floor, applies Quiet/Meeting/Expert suppression and the per-profile sound policy), quiet.py (Quiet Mode + the bounded §11 undo stack), meeting.py (Meeting Mode), mastery.py (per-verb use counter that offers a step-down once at threshold, no nagging), history.py (bounded, filterable announcement history with token-value redaction on by default), explain.py (“Why did QUILL say that?” trace → plain copyable text), safe_mode.py (QUILL_SAFE_MODE detection + non-destructive scoped resets for verb/chord/all), feedback_tuning.py (local Too Much / Too Little / Just Right store with a gentle one-shot suggestion), task_profiles.py (opt-in, default-off per-extension profile suggestions), import_export.py (.quill-verbosity-profile.json import/export — strictly data, no code-execution path, schema-checked), and storage.py (atomic verbosity_custom.json read/write that degrades to empty defaults on a corrupt file). Tests: 11 new tests/unit/core/test_verbosity_*.py modules (180 verbosity cases total; engine coverage 96%). assistant_panel.py/ai_hub_dialog.py +6 each (GATE-11 rebaselined). Partial progress on §9/§10/§11/§24-§27/§29/§31/§35 (#371/#372/#373/#386/#387/#388/#389/#391/#393/#397) whose chord/badge/dialog halves land with the UI sub-PR.

Verbosity system (core foundation)

  • Verbosity rebuild — pure-domain foundation landed (#361, closes §6/§12/§15: #368, #374, #377). New wx-free, strict-typed quill/core/verbosity/ package, the foundation the per-verb verbosity rebuild (docs/planning/verbosity-system.md) builds on. No user-facing behavior changes yet — the engine, runtime modes (Quiet/Meeting), and UI land in later sub-PRs. Modules: channels.py (Channel(Flag) with the always-on VISUAL accessibility floor + route_channels), tokens.py (TokenSpec + the twelve engine filters; no custom filters — the data-only security boundary), parser.py (strict {name} / ${filter:name} / ${filter:arg:name} parser that returns structured errors instead of raising, plus §13 validate() with token allowlist, filter-type checking, and the spoken summary), profiles.py (built-in Beginner/Normal/Expert/Quiet + CustomProfile JSON round-trip, and active_profile() — the first real consumer of the legacy announcement_verbosity knob, mapping minimal/normal/verbose onto the profile ladder), verbs.py/registry.py (VerbSpec + Severity + the 34-verb §15 catalog and a duplicate-protected VerbRegistry), data_order.py (frozen, hashable DataOrder), and schema.py (draft-07 schemas for settings, custom profiles, QVP packs, and import/export). Tests: tests/unit/core/test_verbosity_*.py (102 cases; parser coverage 99%). The §15 prose says “44 verbs” but enumerates 34; the catalog ships the enumerated 34 and the count is asserted from the catalog itself. Partial progress on §5/§7/§13/§14 (#367/#369/#375/#376) whose UI/engine halves remain open.

Publishing and export

  • DAISY 2.02 text-only talking-book export (#251). New File > Export > DAISY Talking Book turns the current buffer into a DAISY 2.02 text-only book that opens in DAISY software readers and on hardware players (Victor Reader Stream, Plextalk, APH units), or in APH Book Wizard Producer to add TTS audio. A DAISY book is a folder, so the chosen name becomes a folder holding ncc.html (Navigation Control Center with dc:/ncc: metadata and heading links), content.html (XHTML with an id on every readable element), and book.smil (a SMIL 1.0 reading-order container, zero-duration since the book is text-only). Headings become player navigation points; documents with no headings (or whose first heading is below h1) get a synthetic title h1 so navigation stays well-formed; inline Markdown is flattened to readable text. The writer is wx-free and strict-typed in new quill/io/daisy.py (write_daisy_textonly) and reads the live editor buffer, so no save is required first. main_frame.py +63 and main_frame_menu.py +8 (GATE-11 rebaselined). Tests in tests/unit/io/test_daisy.py (well-formed XML, DAISY metadata, heading→SMIL→content reference integrity, inline-markup stripping, synthetic-heading promotion). Completes Wave 1 / Phase 1.

Planning and program management

  • Issue tracker consolidated to a 1.0 plan of record (2026-06-21). docs/planning/roadmap.md was rewritten from a per-issue archive dump into the consolidated QUILL 1.0 Plan of Record, organized by workstream and naming the three sibling-product integrations being folded into QUILL: BITS Whisperer (speech/dictation, brand retained; s:\code\bw), GLOW (accessibility audit + agents; s:\code\glow), and ChapterForge (audio → chaptered audiobook/podcast; c:\code\forum). The GitHub tracker was reconciled to match: open issues dropped from 232 to 80. Closed 152 with pointers to their new home — the 100 verbosity brainstorm addenda (deduplicated into a themed polish backlog in verbosity-system.md, with screen-reader-redundant ideas like typing/command echo and speech-rate knobs recorded as “recommend do not build”), 11 AI placeholders (→ the Agentic AI PRD), 30 Tier-6 documentation stubs (→ a Docs & Tutorials track), 7 content-free FEAT stubs (no recoverable intent; their source planning.md was deleted), localization (already shipped), 3 Linux issues (out of scope), and 3 meta/archive placeholders. The GLOW integration (#528–#534, #593–#598) and BITS Whisperer (#515, #566–#577, #617, #663) stay open as live 1.0 workstreams.

Planning and program management (earlier)

  • Issue tracker consolidated into shipping workstreams. The full text of every open planning/design issue was gathered into consolidated workstream specs under docs/planning/ (verbosity-system.md, roadmap.md, feature-backlog.md) with no content loss; the issues remain open and individually tracked. docs/planning/program-tracker.md is the living tracker — buckets, per-bucket risk/impact/value/effort, priority totals, status, and execution waves — driving every open issue to closure by shipping it.
  • Offline speech & dictation plan (#617). Grounded the offline speech-to-text provider architecture in the current code (at the time, dictation only shelled out to Windows dictation; the vosk/whisper settings were dead stubs) and laid out a bold-but-simple reengineering: one offline-first speech engine, two user verbs (Dictate / Transcribe), an accessible model manager, and captions.

Braille Mode (Phase 3 — proofing and progress)

  • BRF sidecar foundation (BR-015, #238). New quill/core/brf_sidecar.py persists per-file braille-workflow state — last position, proofing progress, page anchors, and notes — alongside a braille file as <name>.brf.quill.json. Reads/writes are atomic (storage.write_json_atomic); the braille file is never modified; malformed sidecars raise a clear BRFSidecarError; a missing sidecar reads as None. Pure, wx-free, strict-typed, with round-trip / atomicity / validation tests in tests/unit/core/test_brf_sidecar.py. Foundation for restore-on-open (#239) and the Proofing submenu (#240).
  • Restore last position on opening a braille file (BR-016, #239). Reopening a .brf/.brl/.pef/.ueb file now returns the caret to where you left off (from the sidecar) and announces it, e.g. “BRF file opened. 87 braille pages detected. Last position: braille page 12, line 14, cell 31.” Restore is skipped in safe mode and when sidecar saving is off; a stale offset past the end of an edited file is clamped. Pure logic in new quill/core/brf_progress.py; the open flow delegates its announcement to a thin main_frame_braille._brf_open_message handler (main_frame.py unchanged, net zero). Tests in tests/unit/core/test_brf_progress.py and tests/unit/ui/test_brf_open_restore.py.
  • Braille > Proofing submenu (BR-017, #240). Eight keyboard commands for braille proofing: Mark Current Page Proofed / Needs Review / Clear Mark, Add Proofing Note, Read Progress Summary, List Proofed Pages, List Pages Needing Review (both jump to the chosen page), and Export Proofing Report (to .txt). All state is written to the per-file sidecar (the braille file is never modified). Pure logic in new quill/core/brf_proofing.py; the submenu and handlers live in new quill/ui/main_frame_braille_phase3.py (BrailleProofingCommandsMixin, MRO-layered like Phase 2; main_frame.py +2 for the import + MRO insertion). Commands degrade gracefully on non-braille or unsaved documents. Tests in tests/unit/core/test_brf_proofing.py and tests/unit/ui/test_braille_proofing.py.
  • BRF layout validator (BR-018, #241). New pure, read-only quill/core/brf_validator.py walks a braille document and emits BRFWarning records across ten categories: line-too-long, page-too-long, page-too-short, missing form feeds, mixed line endings, non-NABCC characters, malformed page-separator indicators, page-numbering gaps/duplicates, running-head inconsistency, and Unicode-braille-as-BRF. Each warning carries offset/line/page, a speakable message, and a severity. The validator never modifies the text. Tests in tests/unit/core/test_brf_validator.py (every category, a clean-document baseline, and the bundled corpus sample). The Validation submenu + Warnings List dialog is #242.
  • Braille > Validation submenu (BR-019, #242). Four commands wire the validator (#241) into the editor: Validate BRF Layout (runs the validator and opens an accessible Warnings List — choose a warning to jump to it), Next Warning and Previous Warning (step the caret through findings, announcing “Warning N of M: …”), and Warnings Summary (total count and top three categories). Added to the Phase 3 mixin (BrailleProofingCommandsMixin) — no new MRO insertion; the Warnings List is a stock wx.SingleChoiceDialog registered as native in the dialog inventory. Tests in tests/unit/ui/test_braille_validation.py. With this, Braille proofing and validation (Phases 3 and 4) are complete.
  • Selection-aware braille back-translation (BR-023, #246). Back-Translate (draft) (Braille > Translation) now back-translates the selected braille passage when there is a selection, and the whole document otherwise, announcing which it used (“Back-translation draft from selection/document. N words. Review against the BRF.”). This completes the back-translation deliverable of #246 — letting a proofreader recover the source text of a single passage. Tests in tests/unit/ui/test_braille_translation.py. (The separate “compare back-translation with source” feature was descoped; per-page source linking remains out of scope, needing a cross-format Source-Position model.)

Speech and dictation

  • Dictation engine setting made honest (Speech wave S0, #617). settings.dictation_engine moved to the offline/windows/cloud model with default windows (the only functional engine today); legacy vosk/whisper values migrate to offline to preserve local-engine intent. The dead _transcribe_audio local-recognizer stub (no audio capture was ever wired up) was removed, and quill/core/dictation.py now documents plainly that dictation drives the OS dictation panel until the offline engine lands. No change to dictation behavior; settings.py stayed at its GATE-11 budget. Tests in tests/unit/core/test_dictation.py and test_settings_migration.py.

  • Offline speech-to-text foundation (Speech wave S1, #617). New pure, wx-free quill/core/speech/ package: a typed provider protocol + data model (provider.py — SpeechToTextProvider, model/segment/result records), TXT/SRT/VTT/JSON output formatters (formatters.py), an atomic installed-model store (models.py), a lazy provider registry that skips unavailable/broken providers (registry.py), and a curated whisper.cpp model catalog with accessible size/accuracy/speed descriptions (catalog.py, recommending the Small model). No heavy dependencies and nothing imported at startup; the whisper.cpp provider, model-manager UI, and transcription/dictation flows build on this in S2–S3. Tests in tests/unit/core/speech/.

  • Offline transcription + model manager (Speech wave S2, #617). A new AI > Speech menu makes private, on-device transcription discoverable: Manage Speech Models… (download or remove whisper.cpp models — sizes shown, downloaded over verified HTTPS from the Hugging Face Hub whisper.cpp repo, blocked in Safe Mode) and Transcribe Audio or Video (Offline)… (pick a file; QUILL transcribes locally on a background thread and opens the draft transcript in a new document). The WhisperCppProvider drives a whisper.cpp CLI via run_subprocess_safely with a narrow executable allowlist; the new outbound model download is recorded in the network-egress audit. Logic lives in quill/core/speech/ + quill/ui/main_frame_speech.py (SpeechCommandsMixin); the two stock dialogs are registered native in the dialog inventory. Requires the offline speech engine (see below) and a downloaded model. Tests in tests/unit/core/speech/ and tests/unit/ui/test_speech_commands.py.

  • Offline dictation, captions, microphone selection, and a bundled engine (Speech wave S3, #617). AI > Speech gains: Generate Captions (Offline)… (transcribe with timestamps, save as SRT/VTT), Dictate (Offline) (push-to-talk: speak into the document at the cursor as one undoable edit; bound to QUILL Key + Shift+D, with distinct start/stop earcons via the transcription_started/transcription_stopped sound events and status-bar state), and Dictation Microphone… (choose the input device; saved in <data>/speech-settings.json). Mic capture uses the optional sounddevice extra (quill[speech]), lazily imported with a clear fallback to Windows dictation. The whisper.cpp engine is now a distributable component: a new installer component (“offline speech engine (whisper.cpp)”) installs it to tools\speech\whispercpp, the build script gains --whisper-dir staging, and resolve_whisper_executable searches the QUILL-managed locations ({QUILL_APP_ROOT}/tools/speech/whispercpp, <data>/speech-engine) before PATH — so users no longer install whisper.cpp separately or edit PATH. New quill/core/speech/capture.py (+ device enumeration), quill/ui/main_frame_speech.py commands, keymap binding, and tests; the speech commands are registered for the command palette.

  • Transcript formats and speaker attribution (Speech wave S3, #617). Transcribe (Offline) now asks for a transcript format — Plain text, Markdown, or HTML (captions remain SRT/VTT) — via new formatters.to_markdown/to_html (the existing to_plain_text gains speaker support). Speaker attribution (“who is speaking when”) is produced offline with whisper.cpp tinydiarize: the catalog adds a “Small English with speaker detection” model (small.en-tdrz), TranscriptionRequest.diarize adds the -tdrz flag, the parser turns [SPEAKER_TURN] markers into Speaker 1 / Speaker 2 labels on each TranscriptionSegment, and transcripts render those labels (bold in Markdown/HTML). Transcribe auto-enables diarization when the speaker model is installed. sounddevice is now bundled in the Windows build (added to the bundled dependency groups), so offline dictation works out of the box. Tests in tests/unit/core/speech/test_formatters.py and test_whispercpp.py.

  • Faster Whisper engine (Speech wave S4, #617). Added an optional, GPU-aware second offline engine. New quill/core/speech/providers/fasterwhisper.py (FasterWhisperProvider) loads a CTranslate2 model directory in-process, auto-selecting CUDA/float16 when a GPU is visible and CPU/int8 otherwise (pick_device_and_compute), and maps library segments to QUILL’s TranscriptionSegment (segments_from_faster_whisper, unit-tested).
